Only documented mode switches are advertised; unsupported explicit requests fail clearly. Known flags at source level: Eden `-f -g`; Cemu `-f -g`; Dolphin `-C Dolphin.Display.Fullscreen=True/False` (verify specific build); PCSX2 `-fullscreen/-nofullscreen`; DuckStation same; PPSSPP `--fullscreen/--windowed`; RetroArch `-f`. MAME/RPCS3/other unsupported explicit modes are **not faked**. Legacy boolean imports and library JSON remain compatible. Import stores each selected mode with each game using the existing manual library.
- **Eden input profiles:** optional sanitized `-input-profile <name>` for player 0 on a per-game import; no controller driver installation, no emulator settings rewriting. RetroArch core files must be existing, regular, nonlinked, local DLLs; emulator import now refuses work during Gaming. A FlaUI discovery fixture, command-builder tests, import tests and linked-core refusal fixtures have been added; **NOT RUN**. No evidence yet that a physical controller, emulator window focus, fullscreen return or emulator version actually works.
- **AI/Unsloth dataset correctness (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED / NOT RUN):** the existing first-party chat-template and MinHash near-duplicate analyzer already existed; this increment additionally recognizes OpenAI multimodal content arrays and separates nontext segments from genuinely empty text. Only text portions enter the existing approximate char/4 token histogram, and nontext media is explicitly counted as a quality issue. Two focused xUnit fixtures cover mixed text/image and image-only turns. Real model-specific tokenization, tokenization-aware truncation and training throughput remain open, and no multimodal training support is claimed.
- **Previously in this continuation:** copy-only, owner-consented download-to-staging handoff requires SHA-256 independently provided at download creation, never app-calculated checksum alone. All staged downloads and local ROM scans are checked for unsafe linked paths; no installer, downloaded executable or ROM is executed or distributed automatically.

**Competitive parity is an acceptance target, not an achieved quality ranking.** Benchmark only against reproducible hardware, content, tasks and failure cases, and track demonstrated parity separately from unimplemented behavior. Reference behavior, without copying incompatible licensed components:

| Reference | User-visible behavior PaRuX should match or exceed | Current proof / next software gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Playnite | Single library for stores, portable games and emulators; true controller-first TV grid, title art, filters, alternate installations, reliable game exit and session times | Library/titles/import/console code exists; multi-store artwork, TV focus/keyboard regressions, per-emulator runtime and return-to-Home still open |
| Hydra | Explicit queued/resumable downloads, progress, ownership, useful metadata and save management | First-party HTTP queue, SHA stages, save snapshots exist; recoverable official installer/store transaction, metadata, stable restart resume and integrity provenance still open. Torrent module is OPTIONAL/owner-selected for lawful sources and cannot silently index unlicensed content |
| FitGirl-style/local custom installers | Discover already-owned local/portable/nonstandard game trees and explicit installers without breaking folder layout; handle installer progress/recovery safely | Folder inspector and manual executable imports exist; trustworthy publisher provenance, install rollback, uninstall/recovery and multi-install bookkeeping are not complete. No crack, DRM bypass or unlicensed source catalogue |
| Unsloth | No-code training, tokenizer-aware dataset validation, GGUF export and quantize, VRAM budget and real model benchmarks | Training/runtimes and quantization services exist; convert/export wiring, imatrix, near-duplicate datasets, actual model quality and memory measurements open |
| Locally Uncensored | Install/open/chat/create/code without terminal; detect and reuse existing backends; clear offline and hardware-fit feedback | PaRuX onboarding/runtime/model/media/chat components exist; polish of first-use journey, long-media recovery, realistic dual-GPU and fallback tests open |
| Codex | Safe reviewed diffs, real code review, project-aware agents, approval/sandbox, browser/computer use, GUI tests and application integration | Native coding/apply_patch, agents and Computer Use code exist; dedicated diff review UX, durable agent verification, true app-level E2E and cross-app success evidence open |

**Cross-domain invariants:** Chat is Home; Gaming hard-off means no active speech listener, GPU inference, model discovery, downloads or hidden game polling that competes with gameplay. Voice is OFF during gaming and opt-in outside it. Raw/HID gamepads and ViGEm/HidHide require verified device ownership and deduplication; never install unsigned drivers. Graphics modes, emulator input, source authenticity and installer execution require explicit user choice with rollback. No third-party code is relabeled first-party; preserve notices, never index unauthorized games/ROMs/BIOS/keys.

**Next delivery order:** (1) run the current Windows GOS build+xUnit+FlaUI gate and fix real failures; (2) emulator version/argument and fullscreen/return-to-Home hardware acceptance, installer crash/rollback; (3) controller opt-in input forwarding/dedup + driver acceptance; (4) licensed store metadata, game covers and saves with integrity; (5) AI diff-review, GGUF conversion/quantization, tokenized dataset quality and realistic quality/throughput gates; (6) end-to-end UI/accessibility/multi-monitor/VRAM/FPS/packaging gates. The last item cannot be reported as the *only* remaining work until source gaps 2–5 are actually closed.

Official/reference CLI and feature documentation: https://github.com/UzuCore/eden/blob/master/docs/user/CommandLine.md ; https://pcsx2.net/docs/advanced/cli/ ; https://github.com/stenzek/duckstation/wiki/Command-Line-Arguments ; https://www.ppsspp.org/docs/reference/command-line/ ; https://github.com/dolphin-emu/dolphin/blob/master/Readme.md ; https://github.com/cemu-project/Cemu/blob/main/src/config/LaunchSettings.cpp ; https://playnite.link/ ; https://github.com/hydralauncher/hydra ; https://unsloth.ai/docs ; https://github.com/PurpleDoubleD/locally-uncensored .

**2026-11-01 resumed development checkpoint (post-Windows-gate; SOURCE IMPLEMENTED / NOT RUN):** On public `main`, hardened GOS-07 download filesystem admission by rechecking the local destination and partial/resume sidecars at transfer, file open and publication, rejecting existing/dangling symlinks and mapped network volumes. Added an owner-only ID-based, read-only post-download package inspection handoff: Core remembers the exact non-overwriting published file *in memory only*, invokes the existing inspector, rechecks the original download SHA-256, and exposes an IT/EN desktop action. No arbitrary client path, installer execution, publication trust assertion, or persisted absolute user path. Added synthetic tests for linked/dangling sidecars, byte-change refusal and history privacy. Follow-up GOS-07 source connects completed downloads to the existing integrity-checked, **copy-only** managed staging through an explicit owner-confirmed IT/EN UI action and owner-only opaque-ID API; a user-supplied SHA-256 at the ORIGINAL download request is mandatory, never merely the app-computed checksum. Tests cover successful checked copies, missing expected hash, mutations, Gaming refusal and non-owner access. GOS-11 emulator scanning now refuses symlink/junction ancestry and mapped network volumes, with a synthetic linked-parent regression. Added a non-mutating FlaUI discovery check for download UI controls. **Build, xUnit and FlaUI for these new commits NOT RUN**, and no Windows physical hardware result is implied. This increment does not finish GOS-07: publisher/license verification, official installer/store adapters, transactional install/uninstall, download resume across process restart, verified UX, and GOS-04/05/08/09/10 remain open.

## GOS — PaRuX AI & Gaming OS

**2026-11-01 Windows verification and increments (single source of truth; read before the older NOT RUN notes below):** On Windows 11 / .NET SDK 10 the GOS gate (`tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1 -RunUi`) now runs: Server and WinUI Release builds **BUILD PASSED**, focused xUnit **TESTED** (see `docs/GOS_CONTINUATION_CHECKPOINT.md` for counts), FlaUI Chat Home / Console enter-exit / store discovery / manual library add-launch-cancel-remove **WINDOWS VERIFIED (4/4)**, license inventory gate. Older entries below that say "build NOT RUN" predate this run; everything not listed as verified here stays NOT RUN, and no HARDWARE VERIFIED claim exists (GPU, FPS, VRAM, controller, display, microphone, streaming, installers, emulators).

**GOS-11..14 (added 2026-11-01, Hydra / custom games / downloads / emulators requirement):** see the tracks after GOS-10. Rule that applies to all four: PaRuX manages software the user already has or is entitled to. It does not ship or index repack sources, cracks, keygens, ROMs, BIOS/firmware/keys or DRM bypasses; repacks/portables/homebrew already on the user's disk are treated as ordinary local executables with the same safety checks.

**2026-11-01 public-main checkpoint reconciliation (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Superseded stale branch-only language in `docs/GOS_CONTINUATION_CHECKPOINT.md` with a current public-main source inventory, explicit software-vs-hardware residual gaps, user-directed WIP integration policy and correct local Windows test commands. Added owner-access refusal tests for staged-package create/list/delete and enrolled them in the focused xUnit gate. No test command or independent source review was executed here; commit inclusion is not QA certification.

**2026-11-01 GOS-07 staging inventory UI (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** The same desktop Games workspace now offers a localized **Show staged packages** action and bounded managed-file list. Each managed copy has a separate confirmation before calling the owner-only exact-ID deletion endpoint; original files remain untouched, no background cleanup or driver action. UI displays stable accessible names, short size labels and error feedback; Console TV hides this technical section. Optional FlaUI source asserts the inventory control is present without reading a real disk or deleting files. **Build, xUnit, FlaUI and actual filesystem acceptance NOT RUN**. This is not a licensed store downloader/installer.

**2026-11-01 GOS-07 staged package inventory and explicit cleanup (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Added owner-only GET/DELETE endpoints for managed staged copies. Listing is bounded and restricted to regular PaRuX-named files; deletion validates a 32-hex ID, locks against concurrent staging, refuses Gaming and affects only the selected managed copy, never its original source. xUnit source covers list and exact removal. No background purge or external executable launch. **Windows build and runtime/physical tests NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 GOS-07 UI discoverability regression (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS FLAUI NOT RUN):** The existing optional Game Library FlaUI smoke now locates the independent SHA-256 input and staged-copy button by accessible stable IDs and asserts that staging is disabled with no selected/inspected package. It never supplies a hash, copies a real file or invokes an installer. This test is included in the existing opt-in Windows UI runner, which still has not run in this environment.

**2026-11-01 GOS-07 staging resource budget (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Follow-up to the local package staging transaction: a bounded, stable cross-process OS lock now serializes staging attempts and checks a 16-GiB default staged-storage ceiling, bounded file count, local free-space headroom and linked entries before admitting another copy. It never silently removes previously staged packages. A quota-exhaustion fixture verifies the earlier checked copy remains byte-exact. This prevents unbounded new copies within the managed staging directory; manual cleanup or future explicit owner-managed removal is still needed. **Build, xUnit and real Windows disk pressure/multi-Core tests NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 GOS-07 owner-consented package staging UI (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** The existing desktop Games installer section now retains only the most recently inspected local file in memory, clears stale publisher hashes on new selections, and offers an IT/EN SHA-256 field plus a separate confirmed **Verify and stage copy** action. This calls the new owner-only integrity-checked staging service through the existing typed Core client, reuses the same Gaming/leave-page cancellation and busy state, is not exposed in Console TV mode, and never launches/install/unpacks a package. A full transfer requires a manually pasted independent publisher digest, not the checksum calculated by PaRuX. The checked copy is stored locally with no automatic activation. Physical installer/publisher/rollback/OS acceptance and Windows build/xUnit/FlaUI are **NOT RUN**; official store downloads and installation remain unimplemented.

**2026-11-01 GOS-07 verified local game-package staging (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Added first-party `GamePackageStageService`, owner-only `POST /api/v1/gaming/installers/stage` and typed request/result, separate from the prior read-only SHA-256 inspection. An explicitly provided **independent publisher checksum** is mandatory; the selected local file is streamed into an unguessable app-data temporary name, bounded to 8 GiB, hashed in-flight, compared using constant-time digest equality, then published with a non-overwriting same-directory move only on complete integrity and Gaming-off checks. Failed/cancelled copies delete their partial output; no installer, archive, driver, executable or repack is ever launched or trusted automatically. No new NuGet dependencies or persistent worker. Source tests cover good staging, wrong hash/no output, invalid inputs, Gaming refusal, and cancellation; enrolled in Windows GOS gate. Publisher authenticity, trusted hash-source verification, transactional download, unpack/install rollback, UI/UX and actual game installation are still open. **Build, xUnit, FlaUI, Windows filesystem/AV-lock/disk-full/Gaming tests NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 archive path canonicalization (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Shared ZIP/7z entry processing rejects Windows DOS reserved device names (including extension variants), parent-dot segments, trailing-dot/space names, embedded control characters and ambiguous repeated separators before file materialization. Added pure table fixtures and a ZIP reserved-device regression to the existing local Windows extraction test suite. This complements, but does not replace, root confinement and reparse ancestry checks. It is not proof of race-free extraction or installer trust. **Build, xUnit and Windows ZIP/7z recovery tests NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 GOS-02/03 Quick Chat localization (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** The existing WinUI Quick Chat constructor now applies a shared first-party IT/EN text contract for window title, open/close controls, empty state, placeholder and assistive automation labels/tooltips. It keeps the same bubble lifecycle, single conversation and theme resources without a second localization framework or background service. Added pure bilingual xUnit source and enrolled it in the focused Windows GOS gate. UI tests, screen-reader/keyboard behavior, real language switching and app compilation NOT RUN.

**2026-11-01 GOS-01/03 flat Quick Chat theme (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** The collapsed native WinUI Quick Chat bubble now uses the existing raised-surface, border and primary-text theme resources in place of hardcoded dark gradients, shine and white text; adds a static XAML test enrolled in the existing focused Theme regression gate. No new XAML resources/packages or chat-data migration. This does not assert that the full shell has passed accessibility or physical TV/light/high-contrast acceptance. **Build, focused xUnit, FlaUI, real theme/DPI measurements NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 runtime/archive correctness (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Strengthened the existing first-party ZIP / SharpCompress 7z shared extraction policy: extracted files now stage under randomized sibling temporary names and are renamed without overwrite only after a complete quota-bounded, cancellation-checked copy. A quota failure or duplicate output no longer publishes an incomplete final executable; new xUnit source covers overflow cleanup, duplicate entries and preserved pre-existing bytes. This is an individual-file atomic publication boundary, **not** a whole-archive rollback or system-crash guarantee; 7z fallback stays optional/available, and no dependencies are removed. Release build, ZIP/7z fixtures, disk-full/cancellation and Windows reparse acceptance NOT RUN; see `docs/DEPENDENCY_STRATEGY.md`.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05/06 Playnite process-identity and lifecycle hardening (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** The optional Playnite launcher no longer claims success merely because a process named Playnite exists. It checks the observed process image against the selected local, non-reparse executable (accepting verified Desktop/Fullscreen peers from the same installation), rejects unverifiable lookalikes, and serializes Start with Stop to avoid duplicate owned launches. Explicit Return to AI only disposes PaRuX's handle; it never closes externally running Playnite. Added a deterministic false-positive regression to existing `GamingLauncherSafetyTests` in the local Windows GOS gate. No new dependency, helper polling or game termination. **Build, xUnit, FlaUI and real Playnite process acceptance NOT RUN**; not a guarantee of launcher publisher authenticity or ownership of Steam/GOG/Epic child processes.

**2026-11-01 GOS local-file admission consolidation (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED ON MAIN; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Shared the first-party `GamingLocalFileSafetyPolicy` across the optional executable-input helper, Steam client executable locator and read-only local package SHA-256 preflight. Previously the package inspector did not check linked parent directories or mapped network volumes, and Steam executable selection only rejected linked leaf files. The common bounded policy rejects noncanonical or UNC paths, network volumes, linked ancestors and linked files, with per-use extension rules; package inspection checks its path again after hashing. Added Windows-focused xUnit source for good/bad file paths and optional Windows symlink fixtures; enrolled it in the local GOS gate. No new NuGet packages, drivers, services, silent executable launch or runtime polling. This is path hygiene, **not publisher authenticity, malware detection, atomic Windows file-identity proof or installation**. Full build, xUnit, FlaUI and real Windows filesystem/device checks are NOT RUN; GOS-07/08 and native remapping remain software implementation gaps before physical-only acceptance. See `docs/DEPENDENCY_STRATEGY.md`.

Added 2026-10-31. PaRuX remains a Windows application; OS describes its integrated experience, not a replacement operating system. Existing tracks and their acceptance gates remain in force. Initial development used a local checkout with pre-existing uncommitted work; this publication branch was independently based on public `ab23c4e`. Results from the initial checkout do not certify the publication branch. GOS status describes this initiative, not a claim that existing capabilities are absent. `NOT STARTED`, `IN PROGRESS`, `SOURCE IMPLEMENTED`, `TESTED`, `WINDOWS VERIFIED` and `BLOCKED` describe only the explicitly scoped GOS work. No physical acceptance or performance result is implied by source changes.

**Unified delivery policy (2026-10-31):** PaRuX AI & Gaming OS is the ongoing evolution of the SAME PaRuX Windows application and executable, not a second product, fork, runtime tree or user-data store. Chat, AI Studio, Gaming, Computer Use, models and integrations remain modular workspaces sharing existing services and migration-compatible settings. `main` is the sole intended integrated product line; checkpoint/feature branches are temporary review and verification boundaries, never parallel releases. By explicit product-owner direction, coherent reversible GOS source-only increments may be integrated directly on `main` even before Windows testing, but must be marked SOURCE IMPLEMENTED / NOT RUN and never treated as release-ready. Keep unrelated Computer Use development isolated until a dependency/diff audit, then converge through reviewed increments. The repository is public: no tokens, device paths, private logs or personal screenshots in commits.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05/06 launcher/input ownership hardening (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED on main; ALL TESTS NOT RUN):** The custom input helper now admits only explicitly configured, fully qualified, local, regular non-reparse .exe paths; tracks its exact spawned process, refuses duplicate launches while owned, attempts graceful MainWindow close on Return to AI, and retains failed-stop ownership to prevent fake success or unsafe force-kill. XOutput now shares the bounded local-executable gate and refuses a cancelled start before probing processes. Playnite and custom executable launchers also require local, regular executables; cancelled launches fail closed. Steam Big Picture starts via the already located Steam executable with an argument list, rather than the system-wide steam:// URI handler; a null Process.Start result cannot be reported as success. Added deterministic xUnit sources for cancelled starts, invalid paths and unowned cleanup. This does **not** implement native controller remapping, virtual-driver deduplication, installer download/execution, display restore or controller-first complete Shell. The changes were committed on public main without real Windows/.NET execution; repeat builds, xUnit, FlaUI, live launcher and device tests and security review before release.

**2026-11-01 GOS-06 explicit GOG GALAXY handoff (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED on main; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Added a read-only GOG GALAXY client locator using local registry/default install paths. The source-only owner-authorized GOG launch endpoint accepts a numeric ID, checks that the game is present in the freshly rediscovered installed GOG catalog, then enters the existing transient Gaming session and sends a bounded fixed-argument launch request to the locally installed GALAXY executable; failed handoffs attempt bounded AI rollback. The existing Games/Console discovery view now includes an IT/EN Play via GOG GALAXY button gated by an explicit confirmation and warns that manual Return to AI is required. xUnit source tests cover invalid client paths/IDs and owner authorization, included in the local Windows GOS gate. This does not authenticate GOG account ownership, prove the actual game started or enable automatic return. **Build/xUnit/FlaUI and live GALAXY protocol testing NOT RUN.** Epic launch, store installation/download manager, full native remapping and display restore remain internal implementation gaps.

**2026-11-01 public-repository hygiene and branch inventory:** Removed the already tracked local agent account-switch telemetry log from the current `main` tree and added `.ai/telemetry/` to the root ignore rules. The removal does not erase historical Git blobs or replace secret scanning; no destructive history rewrite was performed. At inspection the repository had five branches including `main`: three GOS branches had zero commits ahead of `main`, while `feature/parux-ai-gaming-os-foundation` diverged (five unique commits containing older UI/Computer Use work). No new parallel branch was created. Never blindly merge this stale branch across newer Computer Use logic; retain it until its unique diff is reviewed or explicitly retired. This status is not a Windows test result.

**2026-11-01 first-party dependency reduction checkpoint (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Added `PaRuX.Contracts.ConsoleNavigationPolicy` and wired existing WinUI Console/TV `KeyDown` to first-party D-pad **and left-thumbstick** focus navigation via Windows `VirtualKey` events; Escape/gamepad B remain disabled while an owner-confirmation/dialog action is busy. A/Enter stay native WinUI actions (no synthesized clicks, driver, virtual XInput, XOutput or polling worker). Added deterministic xUnit source and enrolled it in `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1`. This reduces dependency on controller mappers for **PaRuX UI navigation only**, not universal remapping for games. Navigation and accessibility still require FlaUI + physical controller acceptance.

**2026-11-01 built-in ZIP extraction checkpoint (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** `SafeArchiveExtractor` now chooses the built-in `System.IO.Compression.ZipArchive` reader for ZIP signatures, even when a ZIP has a misleading .7z extension, rather than routing ZIP through `SharpCompress`. It retains SharpCompress for 7z/unsupported formats; therefore **no NuGet package has been removed**. Shared extraction policy rejects Unix ZIP symlink metadata and existing reparse-point/junction ancestors before creating files, preserves the existing real-byte quota, cancellation, root confinement and CreateNew behavior; new xUnit source covers symlinks, pre-existing files and linked parents. Because runtime installs also use this extractor, Windows builds, ZIP/7z fixtures, locked-file/recovery, and compatibility must pass before release; no such executable run occurred here.

**2026-11-01 audited dependency baseline (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS GATE NOT RUN):** Added a reviewed `packaging/dependency-boundaries.json` for the App and Server (19 direct NuGet references, 15 distinct packages), plus a first-party **PowerShell/.NET-only offline check** `tools/audit-dependency-boundaries.ps1` invoked by the existing local GOS Windows gate; no Python/npm/GitHub Actions or new runtime dependency. This flags unreviewed direct package changes and missing vendored llama.cpp license, not transitive vulnerabilities or licensing compliance. A separate read-only source cross-check of the live GitHub project XML, JSON baseline and vendored license path **passed** (19 references, 15 unique, 0 discrepancies). The PowerShell script itself and full Windows builds/xUnit/FlaUI **NOT RUN**. Implementation strategy, licenses and remaining necessary runtimes are recorded in `docs/DEPENDENCY_STRATEGY.md`. Do not confuse an allowlist with removal of dependencies or release readiness.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 first-party suspend default (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** New built-in local/remote Gaming profile presets now select the existing `win32native` provider instead of optional Nyrna. Legacy DTO fallback and existing serialized/user-edited profile settings remain unchanged: there is no automatic provider migration. Explicit `none` now reports suspension/resume as false rather than a fabricated success, and Windows native suspend/resume refuse pre-cancelled calls. Test source covers the new defaults, old JSON fallback, no-op honesty and cancellation, registered in the focused GOS gate. The owned-game-PID admission boundary currently returns null; **native process suspension is not operationally certified** and must remain fail-closed until exact process identity, permissions and hardware acceptance exist. No Windows/.NET execution was performed here.

**2026-11-01 GOS-06 official Epic Games protocol handoff (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED ON MAIN; ALL WINDOWS TESTS NOT RUN):** Added a first-party `EpicGameLaunchProtocol` supporting Epic's documented `com.epicgames.launcher://apps/{URL-encoded-installed-directory}?action=launch&silent=true` form ([Epic Protocol Activation guide](https://assets-unreal2-epic-prod-us2.s3.dualstack.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/original/4X/f/c/4/fc4342c50e1a0abf34943e019ee90bacccc62950.pdf)). The owner-only `POST /api/v1/gaming/library/discover/epic/{id}/launch` accepts only bounded safe app IDs, independently rediscovers an installed game, checks its local path and linked ancestors/network drive, requires a Windows scheme registration, then enters the existing transient Gaming session and requests OS URI activation. The existing Games/TV view now has an IT/EN **Play via Epic Games Launcher** action with explicit confirmation and manual Return to AI; no user path/URI/flags come from the HTTP call. A URI dispatch, even one accepted by the OS, is **not** proof of Epic account ownership, publisher-authentic protocol handler, supported launcher variant or actual game start. Protocol hijacking by an untrusted Windows default handler remains a security consideration; the consent UI warns that the handler is user-configurable. Failure to dispatch triggers bounded AI rollback. No game executable, account/DRM bypass, installer, voice or polling worker is introduced. Added xUnit source for URI encoding, ID rejection, symlink ancestry and owner-only route, enrolled in `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1`. **Build, unit tests, WinUI/FlaUI and real Epic legacy/preview launch acceptance NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 GOS-05/06 input and manual-path follow-up (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** XOutput helper Start/Stop now serialize on the same process ownership lock: no duplicate helper may be launched while PaRuX still owns one, and no spurious success is reported for an unverified same-name external process. Existing configured external XOutput processes are never terminated; unverified variants fail closed. The manual game process start now reuses the stricter shared fully qualified local .exe admission to reject linked *parent* directories, not just linked leaf executables. Added source tests for unverified external helpers and junction-parent manual games. These are regression/safety boundaries, not first-party virtual gamepad remapping or ownership of store-launched games. **Build and live launch/regression tests NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 GOS-04/06 unbounded-store-list UX checkpoint (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** The unified Steam/Epic/GOG library previously hard-limited WinUI rendering to the first 100 discovered titles; `GameLibraryPageWindow` now provides a bounded, dependency-free 50-item window of the entire cached catalog with clamped page indices, Next/Previous buttons, translated page-range status and existing name filter. All 10,000-per-store discovery results remain accessible via pagination; view changes do not rescan disks or poll game hardware. Controls are disabled during scanning; stale state is cleared and the page index resets on new scans/filters. Separate from the existing manual-library paging and compatible with the SAME desktop and Console presentation. xUnit source covers empty, partial, filtered and very large catalogs; FlaUI source covers initial accessible controls. **Build, xUnit, FlaUI, 10,000-title responsiveness, actual controller focus navigation NOT RUN**. This closes the `first 100 only` source/UX gap, not the complete console-shell or multi-store install/download workflow.

**2026-11-01 GOS-04/06 shared store launch-ID policy (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Added the pure first-party `GameStoreLaunchIdPolicy` in Contracts and replaced duplicated Steam/GOG/Epic ID parsing in server handoff paths with a single bounded per-store policy. The desktop/TV discovered-game view reuses exactly that policy to hide unsupported launch actions while keeping the game visible and explaining why the handoff is unavailable (IT/EN). This avoids dead-end confirmation dialogs on unsupported/unsafe game identifiers, rejects URL/command fragments and introduces no new package or runtime. Added independent table-driven xUnit source to the GOS Windows gate. **Tests NOT RUN; launch-ID compatibility with real Epic preview/legacy manifests remains to validate.**

**Baseline observations:** the WinUI shell uses `src/PaRuX.App/Styles/Theme.xaml`, `MainWindow.xaml` and existing Orbit assets. Gaming has a server-side `WorkloadOrchestrator`, profiles and optional launcher/input providers; native input currently has no mapping logic. Existing Gaming hard-off, security, local-only operation, data compatibility and Chat-first workflows are regression boundaries. Existing documentation says local game exit does not automatically return from Gaming; verify current code and real behavior before changing it. The exact full UI and physical-device audit is still outstanding.

**Common gates for each track:** preserve existing behavior, user data and IT/EN, cancellation, security and accessibility; test errors and disabled-module paths. Run targeted build/tests first, then relevant integration and Windows UI gates. Physical controller, display, GPU and streaming verification cannot be replaced by mocks. Record actual results, not historical tests as fresh GOS evidence. GOS checks: focused AccessibilityTests passed 32/32 (these do not validate rendered theme resources); WinUI app Release build passed with 0 errors and 8 warnings after the final theme edit. Visual, physical and broad regression gates remain NOT RUN. Public-base validation: Server and App Release builds passed; 31 targeted tests passed after the minimal baseline test compilation repair; published App + Server FlaUI manual-library add/remove passed 1/1 on isolated data. These checks do not certify the remaining tracks. No FPS or VRAM target is certified.

### GOS-00 — Baseline, audit and migration — IN PROGRESS
- Files: `PARUX_EXECUTION_PLAN.md`, `README.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/WORKLOAD_ORCHESTRATOR.md`, existing tests and configuration stores. Dependencies: current code and existing release tracks.
- Risk: dirty checkout and private data; stale docs; migration regressions. Planned: inventory screens, state formats, features, local gates and migration paths. Implemented: this initial baseline and track inventory; comprehensive audit remains open.

**2026-11-01 GOS-06 GOG and unified catalog increment (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Added `GogGameDiscoveryProvider` reading HKLM/HKCU 32-/64-bit GOG registry game paths in read-only mode, validating numeric IDs and local non-reparse install folders, bounding enumeration to 10,000 entries and deduplicating known game IDs. Added the owner-only `/api/v1/gaming/library/discover/gog` API, desktop client and IT/EN GOG button to the SAME Games workspace, with fixture tests for valid/invalid/cancelled metadata and unauthorized access. The Console/desktop library now also offers one explicit **Detect all installed games** action: independently queries Steam, Epic and GOG, deduplicates per-store ID, reports partial provider failures, filters results and uses cached discovery data when presentation changes. The background game-time state does not poll storefronts. No GOG account/credentials, registry writes, game execution or DRM interaction. **Source/route checks only; .NET build, xUnit, FlaUI and real Windows storefront tests NOT RUN.**

**2026-11-01 GOS-00/06 profile data safety (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** The pre-existing GamingProfileStore swallowed persistence failures and could overwrite corrupt JSON with default profiles. It now writes a temp JSON and flushes before same-directory atomic replacement; Save/Delete update in-memory lists only after a successful disk write. Existing unreadable profile files are left untouched and edits are blocked; the profiles API reports 409 rather than false success. An intentionally empty valid list is preserved. Source regression tests include corrupt-file preservation, reload and delete, explicit empty list, and owner-authorized API conflict. **Tests NOT RUN**; JSON migration/versioning and Windows antivirus/locked-file acceptance remain open.

**2026-11-01 GOS-06 official Steam handoff (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** The existing Games workspace now offers a localized **Play via Steam** action on *discovered Steam items only*, behind a user-confirmation dialog. The owner-only `POST /api/v1/gaming/library/discover/steam/{id}/launch` API validates an app ID as 1–15 decimal digits, confirms that it exists in the current on-disk Steam discovery catalog and that the official Steam client executable exists in the supported installation location; only then does it enter the shared transient Gaming session and launch Steam with argument-array `-applaunch <validated-id>`. No game binary is launched directly, no additional manifest-supplied arguments are accepted, no Steam window is killed/suspended, and no background gameplay process finder is installed. The service explicitly reports a **Steam launch request**, *not* confirmation of the actual game starting; automatic Return to AI is disabled for such delegated games, and a failed process-start triggers bounded rollback. Added numeric-ID/command-injection and unauthorized-API test sources, included via the existing local Gaming test gate. **Build, xUnit, FlaUI, actual Steam client/game handoff and release performance NOT RUN**. GOG/Epic official launch, runtime session ownership/recovery, arbitrary store-path support and install/download/DRM handling remain incomplete.

**2026-11-01 GOS-06 profile multi-Core consistency and validation (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** An OS-shared bounded write lease now surrounds each Gaming-profile Save/Delete read-modify-atomic-replace transaction, re-reading current valid disk state rather than overwriting changes written by another Core process from an obsolete in-memory snapshot. The original file remains untouched when an outside writer corrupts it. New profile writes validate nonblank names, ID length, 1,024-item capacity and ambiguous duplicate names; previously stored longer fields remain readable for migration compatibility. HTTP reports bad input as 400 and name/capacity conflicts as 409. Added deterministic source regressions for two writers, intervening corruption and rejected duplicates. **Build, xUnit and physical simultaneous-Core/antivirus locks NOT RUN**; a fully versioned conflict-resolution API and locked cold-start initialization still require a dedicated design.

This is NOT an install flow.

**2026-11-01 GOS-07 verified-package preflight (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Added `GameInstallerInspectionService`, `GameInstallerInspectionRequest/Result`, and an explicitly consented desktop-owner `POST /api/v1/gaming/installers/inspect`. The existing Games **desktop** workspace offers a local `FileOpenPicker` for .exe/.msi/.msix/.zip/.7z/.rar/.iso files and computes a streaming SHA-256 on a regular, fully qualified non-UNC, non-reparse path, bounded to 8 GiB. It never downloads, extracts, executes or installs a package, does not persist its file path, and explains that a checksum is **not proof of malware safety, copyright/license legitimacy or verified publisher identity**. The service checks Gaming/unknown-recovery state before work, links its cancellation to the existing Runtime Gaming hard-off token and rejects changed files; the desktop client uses a dedicated bounded 10-minute HTTP budget with an explicit Cancel control. Leaving the Games workspace, hiding the window or entering TV Console cancels hashing. Installer actions are unavailable in Console to avoid background game-time CPU/disk contention. xUnit fixture sources cover SHA-256, no file mutation, cancellation, Gaming rejection, unsupported paths and unauthorized API; FlaUI visibility/availability assertions and the focused Windows runner include the new controls. **.NET build, xUnit, FlaUI and physical Windows long-file/Gaming interruption tests NOT RUN**. A real source-verified downloader, publisher-signature verification, archive sandboxing, install rollback, official-store ownership and licensed install handoff remain substantive GOS-07 implementation gaps. No unsupported or non-consensual software installation was enabled.

### GOS-09 — Voice Home and resource lifecycle — IN PROGRESS
- Files: `VoiceConversationController.cs`, speech services and `WorkloadOrchestrator.cs`. Depends on GOS-00/04. Risk: recording during game, leaked resources or broken existing AI recovery.
- Planned: opt-in push-to-talk in Home, stop/unload during Gaming and lazy restoration. Implemented: speech warmup coordination extracted and integrated, with deterministic tests for late completion and retry; its cancellation is not connected to Gaming. No GOS resource-release guarantee is claimed.
- Required tests/acceptance: cancellation/race and device release, real GPU/VRAM and voice process checks during gaming and return. Executed: warmup coordinator tests 5/5 and App Release build; Gaming cancellation and physical resource tests NOT RUN. Open: event wiring, real process/resource ownership evidence.
- Source audit: `SpeechService` checks Gaming before asynchronous work; `SpeechWarmWorker` can start its child before publishing the process handle, so a concurrent stop can miss it. One-shot fallback children are not owned by `StopWorkersForGaming`. The Gaming cancellation token is cancelled before the marker is written, so checking only `GamingMode` does not close this window. Continuous speech has separate process/token coordination that also needs review. These observations are not a runtime reproduction or a claim about actual VRAM release.
- Proposed prerequisite: one admission lifecycle for owned speech children, with a monotonic generation captured per request, atomic start/registration against Gaming entry, cancellation of in-flight work, and verified child exit before reporting quiescence. Invalidate old generations across Gaming exit; do not allow fallback to reacquire admission after cancellation. Define lock ordering around runtime cancellation callbacks and include warm, prewarm, one-shot and continuous paths. Classify status checks and installation separately; do not terminate unrelated processes. No implementation of this contract is claimed.
- Required deterministic tests: barriers before admission, between process start and registration, during active inference and before fallback; Gaming enter/exit and generation rollover, repeated stop, HTTP cancellation, start/kill failure, and child exit not confirmed. Add a Windows integration fixture with an owned child and separately measure actual speech process and GPU resource release. These tests are NOT RUN.

**Additional continuation source change (2026-10-31):** ChatView's existing Gaming state event plus the 2 s marker reconciliation now invoke the same voice UI stop callback for hands-free mode, live microphone recording and read-aloud; a late transcription result is prevented from populating or auto-sending the composer after Gaming begins. The existing `VoiceGamingTransition` observes a single entry without duplicating stop callbacks; no new app, speech service or separate Gaming frontend was created. **SOURCE MODIFIED, BUILD/FlaUI/MIC RELEASE NOT RUN**; this does not prove atomic recorder shutdown, in-flight HTTP cancellation, worker quiescence or VRAM release. A deterministic voice event/recording-lifecycle test and actual Windows microphone/resource check remain required.

**2026-10-31 safety follow-up:** Desktop dictation rechecks the Gaming marker after awaiting speech-status and microphone start; if Gaming began during those awaits, the newly opened microphone is cancelled and no speech warmup is submitted. The transcription path rechecks the marker after stopping the recorder and before analysis/remote-local STT admission; voice UI cleanup cancels the warmup coordinator. These are source guards, **not proof** that worker process startup, recorder disposal, or GPU release is atomic. **Voice remains OFF by default during Gaming**. Do not expose an "allow voice during Gaming" preference until an actual end-to-end implementation and resource acceptance exists: a decorative toggle is prohibited.

**Integration policy update (explicit product-owner direction 2026-10-31):** Continue in a SINGLE product line and fast-forward reviewed/segregated WIP increments into `main` even where Windows build evidence is missing, but label those increments `SOURCE IMPLEMENTED / NOT RUN`, preserve backups and history, and avoid known destructive changes. This overrides the earlier branch-holding recommendation for this integration only; no claim that integration meets Definition of Done. The five GOS source tracks remain partially complete, with existing open entry/exit concurrency races, voice worker admission gaps and broad UI/physical acceptance. Other divergent feature branches are not automatically merged. Do not force-push or introduce GitHub Actions.

**2026-10-31 UI state follow-up (main):** Existing WinUI mic and hands-free Voice buttons now become disabled while the Gaming marker is observed, and re-enable when Gaming exits. Both buttons receive IT/EN UI Automation help text explaining Gaming priority; event subscription and bounded marker reconciliation share the same observer. The controls remain operational outside Gaming. **CODE IMPLEMENTED, NOT BUILT, FLAUI NOT RUN, PHYSICAL MICROPHONE RELEASE NOT VERIFIED.** This is not an opt-in gaming-voice feature: allowing speech during gaming remains intentionally unavailable until the server ownership/lifecycle and performance gates are implemented. The focused Windows runner is source-only and has not been executed.

**2026-10-31 continued main implementation — serialized Gaming transitions (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT TESTED):** After verifying `main` at `8dbec53239297952db234467bbd2648f56a233aa`, `WorkloadOrchestrator` now uses one `SemaphoreSlim` to serialize Gaming entry, exit, Suspend and Resume and prevents new entry in unresolved/error states. Automatic local and Sunshine trigger polling is awaited rather than launched fire-and-forget; a monotonic transition version rejects asynchronous answers from an earlier session. Explicit Return to AI invalidates queued auto triggers immediately and blocks held-trigger reentry until a full inactive observation rearms it. An absent Sunshine provider does not trigger auto exit. Source-only xUnit regressions were added for an exit during blocked launch, duplicate concurrent entry, a delayed trigger after manual exit, and a stale Sunshine disconnect after a new session; the focused Windows test script now includes them. **Static source assertions 9/9 passed; .NET/WinUI build, xUnit, FlaUI, actual Gaming and independent external review NOT RUN** (the environment has no Windows or .NET and cannot clone GitHub directly). The work is integrated on `main` under user-directed WIP policy, not release-certified. It does **not** complete owned game-process suspend/resume, atomic speech child admission, voice/microphone/VRAM quiescence, or whole-system lifecycle coordination with external GamingGuard. Run `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1` on the development Windows PC and review current changes before enabling new automatic game-launch capabilities. Maintain one product line and do not merge unrelated feature branches without an audit.

**2026-10-31 GOS-09 follow-up — managed speech process ownership (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT BUILT):** Added `SpeechProcessAdmission`, shared per `LocalRuntimeService` across resident STT/TTS, continuous TTS and one-shot/status Python children. The service admits a process only when a matching generation and Gaming hard-off permit it; OS `Process.Start` and ownership registration share one lock. Gaming closes admission, invalidates stale requests, kills only tracked owned children and bounds confirmation of process exit (1.2 s per child); an uncertain exit is reported as failure and remains tracked for retry, preventing voice from silently returning after Gaming. The Gaming guard retries unconfirmed speech shutdown, and orchestrator entry refuses to claim ready-to-game when a managed speech child has not confirmed exit. Both explicitly reopen lazy speech admission only when Gaming ends and no tracked child remains; previous-generation requests cannot reacquire it. Desktop voice controls remain disabled by default in Gaming. Speech process start, exit and blocked-generation regression sources added to `SpeechProcessAdmissionTests`; existing focused Windows runner includes them and streaming cancellation tests. **Neither Server/App build nor xUnit, FlaUI, actual microphone release, direct child process cleanup or GPU/VRAM measurements were run here.** `Process.WaitForExit` proves only managed child exit when it succeeds, **not** VRAM release, universal microphone release, all possible Python grandchildren or no gameplay interference. Review locking, cancellation callbacks, warm/streaming fallback and robustness under repeated transitions on the Windows developer PC before release. Status remains IN PROGRESS.

**2026-10-31 GOS-06 follow-up — multi-process manual library write safety (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT BUILT):** `ManualGameLibrary` now takes a bounded OS file-share lease around each read-modify-atomic-replace operation, preventing independent PaRuX Core instances from silently overwriting each other's additions/removals. It retains a tiny lock file to preserve cross-process lock identity, with no change to version-1 library schema or original data. Added concurrent multi-instance and duplicate-executable regression source. **Unit and UI tests NOT RUN.** Official launchers, imports, multi-provider catalogs, installer and player ownership remain unimplemented GOS deliverables.

**Explicit remaining implementation gaps before a physical-only acceptance stage:** GOS-04 Console Shell (controller-first navigation), GOS-05 native mapping and deduplicated virtual input, GOS-06 official Steam/Epic/GOG providers and safe owned game lifecycle, GOS-07 download/install transaction framework, GOS-08 display/streaming restore, GOS-01 production branding source was subsequently integrated (2026-11-03) but its physical/installer acceptance is NOT COMPLETE; GOS-02/03 comprehensive visual/accessibility audit is also NOT COMPLETE. Do not describe this checkpoint as “only PC tests left”; these are still source implementation tasks. Continue through the canonical dependency order on the one product line.

The opt-in Windows `-RunUi` gate now includes `ManualGameLibraryUiTests` as well as `ChatHomeStarterTests`. **FlaUI NOT RUN**; separate physical store/OS acceptance and genuine launch/import integration remain outstanding.

**2026-10-31 GOS-06/09 continuation on main — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, WINDOWS NOT RUN:** Existing manual game library now has an explicit owner-only `POST /api/v1/gaming/library/{id}/launch` and a real localized WinUI Play action with confirmation. `ManualGameSessionService` enters the SAME orchestrator using a transient native-input/no-launcher/no-suspend profile (does not modify saved gaming profiles), checks the saved local `.exe`, and launches only after consent. When the returned Windows handle exposes matching executable path, PID and nondefault creation time, it monitors that exact process and requests lazy Return to AI after an observed non-bootstrap exit (5s threshold); a monotonically versioned session check prevents old exit notifications from ending a newer session. Otherwise the user must Return to AI manually. Explicit Return is serialized against manual launch and only detaches the monitor; **no process is force-killed, suspended or discovered heuristically**. This is process observation, not comprehensive Steam/Epic/GOG game tracking or automatic Console Home focus. New-install default local profile is manual with launcher `none`; remote Sunshine starts disabled. Saved profiles are not migrated or overwritten by this choice. Playnite Stop releases PaRuX's handle without closing Playnite. XOutput Stop is graceful-only; a failure is reported, never replaced by a silent kill, and the orchestrator refuses to claim successful AI recovery when a provider returns false. Added xUnit source regressions for these defaults, preserving customized profiles, launch-path validation, owner-only API, stale manual exit and failed helper stop; added a FlaUI cancel-confirmation assertion without executing a fixture game. `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1` includes the tests. **Build, xUnit, FlaUI, runtime process ownership, game focus and performance NOT RUN here; no independent external review.** Remaining source work before physical-only gate includes Console Shell, advanced controller input mapping, reliable store ownership beyond manual library, store download/install, GOG, display/streaming recovery and full UI/UX acceptance. This is not a completed Gaming OS.

**2026-11-01 GOS-08 monitor detection and GOS-05 XInput diagnostics (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Added `GamingDisplayTopologyService` with read-only Windows `EnumDisplayMonitors` / `GetMonitorInfoW` / `EnumDisplaySettingsW` calls bounded to 16 displays. It exposes logical monitor ID, primary flag, position, current geometry and reported current refresh (0 when unknown) without EDID serial, HDR/VRR claims or any setting mutation. `GamingControllerStateDto` and an on-demand `NativeXInputProbe.ReadStates` query at most 4 Windows XInput controller slots; pure radial deadzone normalization computes diagnostic stick axes without changing input behavior. Both are desktop-owner-only APIs, requested explicitly through two localized controls in the SAME Games/Console workspace; neither introduces a resident polling thread, driver, virtual XInput device or game-time workload. Source regressions cover bounded topology normalization, unknown refresh, endpoint security, analog extremes/deadzones, and FlaUI control reachability. `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1` now includes the tests. **Server/WinUI build, xUnit, FlaUI, physical multi-monitor, controller mapping, input latency, HDR/VRR and Sunshine tests have NOT RUN.** Reversible display mode changes, controller mapping/virtual backend, double-input avoidance, streaming session verification, focus recovery and safe rollback are still implementation work. Do not claim GOS-05 or GOS-08 complete.

**2026-11-01 GOS-06 profile concurrency completion (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** The persisted Gaming profile store now also takes its cross-process lease during cold-start and preset migration, in addition to Save/Delete. This prevents concurrent Core starts from racing default initialization or overwriting a newer profile during migration. Deterministic regression source added for eight simultaneous initializations. **No Windows build, unit execution or multi-PC storage test run.** Storage migration/versioning and recovery still require acceptance.

**2026-11-01 GOS-04/06 Console Home and AI return (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** The existing localized Games workspace now shows real **AI/Gaming session** status on explicit request and a disabled-until-confirmed **Return to AI** button (reusing the existing owner-authorized `/api/v1/gaming/exit` lifecycle). After consent, it attempts to restore AI resources without terminating any game or Steam and reports refusal; no game-control/background status polling was introduced. Manual/Steam launch actions refresh the shared status UI. In fullscreen Console, the first focus target is an interactive Games control after initial load; Escape/gamepad B cannot tear down fullscreen underneath an active confirmation dialog; technical filesystem editing fields are hidden in TV presentation only and restored without data mutation in desktop mode. Added targeted real-UI FlaUI assertions (not run). These are necessary controller-first entry/exit building blocks, not completion of console gamepad navigation, virtual keyboard or focus recovery after an external game.

**2026-11-01 GOS-06 custom Steam installs (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Added `SteamInstallLocator`, consulting standard local folders and read-only Valve Steam HKCU/HKLM registry paths in both registry views. All candidates must resolve to an existing, local, non-reparse `steam.exe`; registry data is never used as command-line arguments. Steam installed-library discovery, Big Picture launcher and consented Steam game launch now share the locator. Pure fixture source tests cover custom location, path injection, untrusted filenames, missing and UNC paths and feed the Windows focused gate. Neither Windows registry/moved-library installation nor Steam launch was verified here. No silent installer/downloader has been introduced; the Steam `steam://install` protocol has inconsistent historical behavior and cannot justify claiming install completion without actual runtime acceptance.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 fail-closed controller input overlap (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Added `GamingInputSafetyPolicy` and pre-release admission in `WorkloadOrchestrator` before AI GPU workers are touched. Remote profiles selecting XOutput or custom local helper are refused instead of risking duplicate Sunshine/mapper controller input; local profiles selecting Sunshine virtual input are refused. Provider Start methods independently enforce the policy to avoid a bypass. The native provider status name no longer invokes XInput hardware calls: controller slots are queried only through the explicit diagnostic endpoint. Added pure and deterministic transition test sources, including refusal before any helper launch, and included them in `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1`. This does **not** implement physical controller remapping, virtual-driver ownership, double-input deduplication for externally launched mappers or latency testing. Build and Windows tests NOT RUN; unsafe old custom profiles will require user correction when activated.

**2026-11-01 GOS-09 recovery edge case (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** The Core may restart with in-memory `AiReady` while the runtime's persisted Gaming hard-off is still enabled. `WorkloadOrchestrator.GetStatus()` now exposes that real hard-off in `IsGamingActive`, letting the existing Game Library/Console Home offer user-controlled `Return to AI` and show an explicit stale-lock explanation rather than misleadingly disabling recovery. This uses the existing serialized `ExitGamingSessionAsync` and never kills the game. Added deterministic source regression for persisted runtime hard-off after a fresh orchestrator. Build, tests and restart/VRAM/microphone physical acceptance **NOT RUN**.

### GOS-10 — QA, performance, security and release — NOT STARTED
- Files: `tests/`, local `tools/`, security and release documentation. Depends on each applicable track. Risk: false success, privacy leakage, unsupported performance claims or regression in existing features.
- Planned: local Windows regression, threat review, privacy review, migration and comparative direct-game baseline. Implemented: existing testing infrastructure only; no new GOS release acceptance.
- Required tests/acceptance: physical end-to-end hardware matrix, direct-game versus PaRuX FPS/frame-time/CPU/RAM/VRAM and lifecycle measurements, release/package and security checks. Executed: NOT RUN. Open: benchmark protocol, representative games and complete evidence; <2% FPS regression is a target, not a measurement.

### Replacing external tools (Nyrna, XOutput, Playnite, Hydra) — status as of 2026-11-01
- **Nyrna (suspend/resume a game): covered natively, no extra app.** `win32native` suspend provider (default), Suspend/Resume in Settings → Gaming, and Quick Resume/suspend now reachable for the game process PaRuX itself started (PID + creation time + image path re-checked before acting). Deliberately NOT offered: suspending an arbitrary foreign window's process (Nyrna's "any app" mode) — unsafe without ownership. The optional external Nyrna provider stays available. Code from Nyrna (GPL-3.0) is not used. Hardware/game acceptance: NOT RUN.
- **XOutput (map other controllers to a virtual Xbox pad): NOT implementable without a driver.** Windows has no built-in way to create a virtual XInput device; every tool needs a kernel driver (ViGEmBus or similar). What exists: real XInput read, profile (deadzones, trigger threshold, invert, A/B swap) used for diagnostics and Console navigation, calibration, and a read-only SetupAPI probe (`/api/v1/gaming/virtual-controller`) that reports whether ViGEmBus is installed. PaRuX never installs a driver silently and does not forward input yet (`CanForwardInput=false`). Options for the user: (1) keep using the optional external XOutput provider; (2) install ViGEmBus themselves and wait for the forwarding backend (**BLOCKED: needs the driver and a physical controller to verify; ViGEmBus is also no longer maintained upstream**); (3) DirectInput/HID reading for non-XInput pads (**absent, hardware needed**). Owner decided YES (2026-11-01, "integrate it, otherwise optional dependency"). Delivered, all SOURCE IMPLEMENTED + xUnit with fakes, **none run against a driver or a controller**: `GenericPadMapper` (pad with arbitrary button/axis/hat indices to an Xbox 360 report; deadzone, Y inversion, analog/digital triggers; validated user mapping), `ViGEmBusClient` (original C# implementation of the ViGEmBus IOCTL contract, opt-in, fail-closed, IOCTL codes checked against the published values), `HidHideManager` (drives the user-installed HidHideCLI: hides chosen pads and allows PaRuX, writes a journal first, undoes exactly its own changes, recovers after a crash, never touches a user's own HidHide setup). Both components stay **optional dependencies**: ViGEm upstream is archived (2023), HidHide is maintained but its last release is 2024. **Still missing for end-to-end use: a source that reads non-XInput pads (Windows.Gaming.Input RawGameController in the WinUI app, or HID), a forwarding loop with explicit opt-in per profile (it is a polling thread, so it must stay off during Gaming unless the user enables it), mapping UI, and physical verification.** Do not describe this as working support for DualShock/Switch/other pads until a real run proves it.
- **Playnite (library, any executable, launcher front-end): covered** by the universal library, folder import, applications and emulator entries. Playnite plugins/extensions and its metadata scrapers are not replicated.
- **Hydra: covered natively for local library, custom games, HTTP downloads, playtime, favorites; not covered (by design): hosters/repack catalogues, torrent game swarms, cloud profile.** See GOS-11/13.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 HidHide + pad-mapper safety follow-up (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED / WINDOWS NOT RUN):** A corrupt or unreadable HidHide recovery journal now fails closed rather than falsely returning "nothing to restore". Failed CLI apply and incomplete rollback retain a recoverable journal and report the failure accurately; cancellation attempts restoration using an independent token. Generic pad mapping rejects non-finite deadzones and safely ignores negative, unvalidated source indices. Fake CLI and pure regression tests cover partial rollback, retry, tampered journal, canceled apply, invalid mapping. No physical driver, controller, WinUI input forwarding, .NET build/xUnit or performance evidence is asserted by this change. Native non-XInput capture, opt-in forwarding lifecycle, deduplication and mapping UI remain source gaps.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 virtual-pad forwarding lifecycle (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; TEST SOURCES ADDED; NOT RUN):** Added first-party `VirtualPadForwardingSession` with a single host-selected raw controller source and owned optional virtual Xbox pad; caller-confirmed input-isolation gate checked before start and each frame, 8–100 ms bounded sampling, a frozen mapping, neutral report before/after use, automatic unplug on disconnect or loss of isolation, duplicate-start refusal and bounded owner stop. Synthetic xUnit source cases cover denied isolation, explicit stop, disconnect, double-start prevention, loss of isolation and malformed mapping; no Windows/.NET gate was run. This component remains **a building block, not end-to-end XOutput replacement**: a real Windows RawGameController/HID reader, HidHide admission/verified device ownership, user-facing per-game mapping, live lifecycle, latency and driver validation are still missing. It is never automatically started in Gaming.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 Windows raw-controller diagnostic integration (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; BUILD/UI/HARDWARE NOT RUN):** Added `WindowsRawGamepadSource` in the WinUI host using the documented `Windows.Gaming.Input.RawGameController` API. It lists up to 16 connected devices on demand, bounds per-device buttons/axes/switches (64/64/8), selects by a Windows non-roamable connection ID, rechecks that the device still exists before each requested sample, translates native 0..1 axes into -1..1 for the existing mapper, handles hats and rejects nonfinite readings. No permanent watcher/polling starts in Games. The existing Games device panel now has IT/EN controls for discover/select/one-shot read, with FlaUI source assertions for AutomationIds and descriptive names. The existing original C# virtual backend, mapper and forwarding lifecycle sources are linked into WinUI from the Server source tree (no new NuGet package). **Still not complete:** physically proving that RawGameController's ID maps to HidHide's specific HID instance path; safe opt-in per-profile virtual forwarding UI and lifecycle; driver/hardware tests. Do not enable virtual input until physical ownership and deduplication can be established. Windows build, xUnit and FlaUI remain NOT RUN.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 per-device mapping persistence and editor (SOURCE + TESTS ADDED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Added original C# `RawPadMappingStore` with SHA-256-hashed physical connection IDs (raw identity never persisted), bounded JSON, version 1, validation, flush + atomic same-directory publication, and fail-closed preservation of damaged existing mappings. The WinUI Games devices pane now exposes localized, manual A/B button indices, stick/trigger axis indices, Y inversion, D-pad hat and deadzone controls plus explicit Load/Save actions; samples show pressed button indices and axis index/value pairs so the user can identify the correct mapping. Saved maps remain data only, NOT automatically forwarded. Added store tests for privacy, isolation between devices, atomic overwrite refusal after corruption and invalid inputs; included forwarding/store tests in `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1`; expanded FlaUI AutomationId smoke. **No .NET build, xUnit, FlaUI, device/driver, game input latency or HidHide identity binding performed in this environment.** Current source gap: physical RawGameController-to-HidHide identity proof, confirmed no-double-input admission, opt-in per-game forwarding lifecycle, real/alternative device backend, robust crash cleanup. Physical-only acceptance must not be claimed yet.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 persisted-Gaming recovery guard (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; NOT RUN):** Startup HidHide journal replay is now deferred if the runtime still reports a persisted Gaming hard-off; it reports the pending journal rather than unveiling physical input while an older game/virtual controller could still be active. The owner must resolve Gaming and use the explicit recovery path. This keeps recovery fail-closed but needs real crash/restart/driver verification.

**2026-11-01 GOS-05 HidHide recovery lifecycle (SOURCE + TEST SOURCES ADDED; NOT RUN):** Found that the prior journal restore routine was never called at Core startup. Real app startup now attempts recovery only if a PaRuX-owned journal exists, with a 15-second cancellation budget and logged warning on failure; the journal is preserved for retry. The desktop-owner API provides read-only `recoveryRequired` status without device IDs, and a bounded, explicit `POST /api/v1/gaming/hidhide/recover` refused while Gaming owns the machine. The existing Games device panel now offers an IT/EN status check and confirmation dialog before restoring ONLY journaled PaRuX changes. Added fake-CLI journal state assertions and FlaUI control reachability. **No real HidHide CLI, driver, shutdown, Windows build, xUnit or FlaUI execution**; interruption during multi-step recovery, hung drivers and live ownership/anti-double-input remain acceptance blockers. The forwarder is not activated automatically.

**2026-11-01 GOS-13 partial-file security follow-up (SOURCE + TEST SOURCES ADDED; NOT RUN):** The native downloader now rejects a pre-existing linked `.parux-part` file and refuses unsolicited HTTP 206 responses without a preceding nonzero Range request. A local-host regression simulates a non-conforming host sending 206 to a full GET; no file is published. Actual symlink/reparse race, locked-volume and multi-Core destination collisions still require Windows acceptance/security review.

**2026-11-01 GOS-11 native user collections (SOURCE + TEST SOURCES; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** The unified title-state overlay gained additive, bounded local named collections (up to 12 per title, each up to 40 characters), distinct from descriptive tags. Owner-authenticated `PUT /api/v1/gaming/titles/{key}/collections` and optional `collection=` filter reuse existing validation and owner permissions. The Games workspace offers an Italian/English per-title collections editor and explicit exact-name filter; technical editing stays out of TV presentation. Old `game-titles.json` files deserialize without migration or data loss. Added server fixture tests for persistence, case-insensitive deduplication, filtering, invalid input and legacy JSON, plus FlaUI control-reachability source. No .NET build/xUnit/FlaUI ran in this environment. **GOS-11 still open:** cached official artwork/covers, local save backup, additional storefronts, source-backed data migration and multi-Core overlay contention checks.

**2026-11-01 GOS-11 multi-Core title overlay integrity (SOURCE + xUnit FIXTURES; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** `GameTitleStore` now uses an OS-coordinated, bounded file-share lease for every write; mutations reload the latest valid on-disk state under the lease to preserve concurrent favorites, tags, collections, preferred installation and observed playtime updates across independent Core instances. It flushes a new temporary JSON before same-directory atomic replacement, refreshes snapshots on read, and refuses corrupted/invalid external changes rather than overwriting them. Added deterministic two-Core stale-snapshot and intervening-corruption regressions. No physical multi-process/Windows antivirus/locked-volume acceptance or .NET build was run.

**2026-11-01 GOS-11 local save backups (SOURCE + UI + TEST FIXTURES; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Added a first-party local game-save backup service plus owner-authorized list/create/verify endpoints and a bilingual panel inside Advanced Game Management. The desktop owner selects the save folder, confirms, can cancel, and verifies each saved file SHA-256 plus the archive checksum after an explicit action. This service stores a ZIP and versioned manifest only under PaRuX AppData; it does **not** restore, delete or modify original saves, discover game files on its own or run backups during Gaming. Path/size limits include 512 files, 512 MiB, nesting depth 8, 100 archived snapshots, local non-linked folders only, bounded traversal, no duplicate ZIP entries, and rejected self-referential source/backup paths. Test sources cover synthetic backups, corruption, idle gating, invalid labels and folder limits. The dedicated server xUnit class was added to `tools/test-gos-checkpoint.ps1`; the existing advanced FlaUI smoke checks the new controls. **Build/xUnit/FlaUI and physical Windows filesystem/Gaming interruption tests NOT RUN.** There is deliberately no save restore/overwrite feature without a separate reversible recovery design.
