### GOS-13 — Download manager (native, modular) — IN PROGRESS (HTTP + embedded BitTorrent source WIP)
**2026-11-05 Gaming hard-off after limiter wait (MAIN SOURCE WIP):** Native single/multi-file transfers now recheck the runtime Gaming gate immediately after rate-limit waits before each payload write and again before flush. Synthetic tests inject Gaming during the limiter callback; authored only, NOT RUN. This narrows a resource interference race but is not a cross-thread atomic guarantee; keep GOS-13 PARTIAL.

**2026-11-05 final-copy length validation (MAIN SOURCE WIP):** Native v1 torrent output now bounds every copied chunk against declared metadata and rejects short single-file stage reads at EOF; ZIP-packaged multi-file output also checks each entry before writing. A new source-only accounting regression is authored but NOT RUN. Still require Windows restore/build, real local peer, cancellation/race and full security acceptance. No release.
**2026-11-05 pinned API correction (SOURCE WIP):** Checked `release-v3.0.2` upstream and fixed identified `EngineSettings` immutability, speed-property names, runtime update and magnet metadata return-type mismatches in `EmbeddedTorrentTransport`. The original source could not compile as written against 3.0.2. New code is source-only; restore/build/peer transfer in real Windows environment still mandatory before marking the native engine DONE.


**2026-11-05 incremental source update — native restart (NO WINDOWS BUILD/PEER PROOF):** A `NativeTorrentRecoveryStore` now stores the owner-queued integrated-native magnet or local .torrent source, GUID job identity, destination and progress in a bounded Windows CurrentUser-DPAPI-encrypted sidecar; HTTP signed URLs and external qBittorrent sources are intentionally excluded. Recovered jobs appear **PAUSED**, require an explicit click to resume and disable post-recovery auto-install; the old staging location is reused, but verified piece rehash after process restart must be observed on an actual MonoTorrent peer fixture. The existing in-process v1 multi-file support packages safe files into a user-named ZIP without automatic installation and without publishing raw game folders. The WinUI wording now matches this bounded capability. New synthetic DPAPI source tests are authored-not-run. **Do not promote GOS-13 to DONE before Windows NuGet/build, peer fixtures, interruption, privacy, bandwidth and full UI acceptance.**


**2026-11-05 follow-up (MAIN SOURCE WIP; NO WINDOWS BUILD):** Source integrity tightened without changing the native MonoTorrent feature set: a selected local `.torrent` is now read under one bounded read/share lease, the v1 info-hash for transfer is computed from those exact bytes (no second file-open window), duplicate critical bencode keys and duplicate top-level `info` are refused, and existing native-torrent stage trees are checked for links and bounded traversals **before** resuming peer writes. New synthetic tests cover replaced sources and unsafe resume staging. Still no real peer transfer proof; multirecord/multi-file folders and automatic execution remain unsupported.


**2026-11-05 continued GOS-07/13 owner-authorized post-download auto-deploy — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED ON MAIN / TESTS AUTHORED-NOT-RUN:** New `PortableGameInstallService` is registered in Core and called automatically from a job **only when** the owner explicitly checks `AutoInstallPortable` at enqueue with an independently acquired 64-hex SHA-256, a resulting `.zip` filename, relative portable `.exe` path (not setup/install/patch/crack) and game title. Validated HTTP, magnet and local `.torrent` inputs all share the same post-processing code; the current BitTorrent transport itself remains v1 **single-file only**. The normal queue hashes the completed downloaded file against the owner-provided SHA-256 before publication; the post-install service re-verifies and read-locks the archive, preflights ZIP entry bounds/duplicate paths/size/no links, calls PaRuX's existing safe extractor to a managed partial folder, commits via same-volume directory rename and adds the game to `ManualGameLibrary`. This is a **portable deployment**, not launching any EXE/script/MSI. Non-success attempts rollback their managed partials and preserve the downloaded archive; download completion and portable install state (pending/installing/installed/interrupted/failed) are separate. IT/EN Download UI has an opt-in checkbox and bounded title/executable fields, confirmation and job status; tests include in-memory synthetic ZIPs and a loopback HTTP->SHA->deploy->library path, plus default-off FlaUI assertion. **No .NET/Windows/FlaUI/runtime test executed in this cloud session.** End-to-end checks must cover real torrent downloads, cancellation and hard-off during extract, malicious/racing archives, disk-full/OS crash recovery, Store installer permissions and 13-inch/controller UI.

**Not included / do not claim:** no program is silently executed; user-supplied installer/setup EXEs, MSI/MSIX, 7z/RAR/ISO, split archives and multi-file torrent game folders have no automated install adapter yet. No repack catalogues, DRM bypass, rights validation from BitTorrent metadata, arbitrary extracted scripts, automatic permission elevation or unattended EULAs. Download bytes matching an SHA-256 supplied by the user is not publisher-signature verification. Before release 3.1.0 the pinned MonoTorrent v3.0.2 dependency and all new source require clean Windows restore/build/testing and security review.



**2026-11-05 owner-directed Hydra-like BitTorrent continuation (MAIN SOURCE IMPLEMENTED / NOT COMPILED / TESTS AUTHORED-NOT-RUN):** Existing `GameDownloadManager` HTTPS and optional qBittorrent WebUI adapter were audited; in-process `EmbeddedTorrentTransport` was added using a pinned MonoTorrent 3.0.2 MIT NuGet dependency (bundled library, no separate torrent-client application). Its transport is the default for owner-supplied v1 single-file `magnet:?xt=urn:btih:` or local `.torrent` when no optional qBittorrent override is configured. A job pins its selected transport at enqueue to avoid mid-transfer switching. Shared download queue retains existing priority, pause/resume/cancel, Gaming hard-off, expected SHA-256 and non-overwrite publication. Native transport checks info-hash/bencoded metadata, limits metadata size, rejects multifile/unsafe names, verifies stage boundaries/free space, stops peers before moving checked bytes to PaRuX's private part file, and does not seed after completion. UPnP/NAT mapping, LAN peer discovery, automatic jobs, torrent source catalogues and installer execution remain disabled. Bandwidth cap is provided to the engine and updated from the existing UI limit. The Windows Games Download panel now chooses HTTPS/magnet/local `.torrent`, offers a file picker and explicit peer-IP exposure warning, progress bars and idle-only UI refresh; desktop-owner status route `/api/v1/gaming/downloads/torrent-engine` reports which transport new jobs use. Corporate Lite remains no-network. MIT notice inventoried in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.

**Important scope boundaries:** This is *source only*, not a working/released BitTorrent feature. Real MonoTorrent API compatibility and package restore, fresh Windows Server/App build, synthetic xUnit, offline fake-peer and real legal-source peer transfer, client shutdown, upload rate, cancellation vs publication, safe restart, controller/13-inch/IT-EN UI, full FlaUI regression and dependency/vulnerability/license inventory all need execution. Torrent v2/hybrid, multifile folder atomic publish, stable restart resume, peer/seeder counts and optional per-file selection are **MISSING** and must not be advertised as done. No repack/crack/DRM-bypass catalogue or download recommendation sources. Continue GOS-13 with real tests and fixes before changing its status to DONE or shipping 3.1.0.


- Existing base: `ModelDownloadService` (AI models: HTTP resume with If-Range/Content-Range, SHA-256, retry/backoff, bandwidth limit, free-space check) and `GamePackageStageService`. Priority: P1.
- Done (SOURCE + 22 xUnit against a local HTTP server): `GameDownloadManager` + `/api/v1/gaming/downloads*`: https (or loopback http) addresses supplied by the user, no catalogue; Range resume with If-Range/ETag and Content-Range check; retry with backoff on network errors and 408/429/5xx; optional SHA-256 (mismatch deletes the file; without one the file is reported as not verified); bandwidth limit; pause/resume/cancel (cancel deletes the partial file); queue with priority and concurrency; free-space and size cap; unique file names, never overwrites; redirects obey the same rules; history without query strings; automatic pause while a game runs and explicit resume through `IGamingResumable` (no polling). Open: UI (Downloads view), per-profile policy, install hand-off, BitTorrent.
- Historical plan (2026-11-01; superseded by current 2026-11-05 GOS-13 source increment): generalise queued/running/paused/failed/completed/cancelled states across first-party HTTPS and optional user-supplied lawful torrent sources without any catalogues. Built-in MonoTorrent has now been **implemented in source as unverified WIP**, not accepted, tested or released. Its scope remains single-file v1 until Windows and security gates pass.
- Acceptance: pause/resume/cancel/limit/priority/concurrency covered by deterministic xUnit with a local test HTTP server; temp files cleaned; nothing runs during Gaming.

**2026-11-01 GOS-13 resume-source identity (SOURCE + TESTS ADDED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Partial HTTP game downloads now have an adjacent versioned SHA-256 source fingerprint (full URL plus requested expected checksum). The persisted identity is a digest only, never plaintext query credentials; an existing partial without a matching fingerprint is discarded before issuing a ranged request. A matching URL/checksum retains resumable bytes, including after app restart. The sidecar is cleared on completion and cancel. Added local-server test sources for a stale foreign partial and restart-safe resume without query-token persistence. Signed URL rotation intentionally restarts from byte zero. Still needs xUnit, Windows crash/disk-full testing, multi-process destination ownership and broader security review; not release-certified.

**2026-11-01 GOS-13 publication/pause ordering correction (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** After checksum verification, the same-directory final rename now shares the download-manager lock with Pause/Cancel. Token cancellation, disposed ownership, explicit user pause, canceled state and Gaming Mode are checked before the non-overwriting publication, so cancellation accepted before the commit cannot be overwritten by a late Completed report. A rename completed first remains a completed file; subsequent remove affects only download history. This tightens a source race but does not claim absolute filesystem/network atomicity or live Windows concurrency proof. Existing pause/cancel/checksum xUnit and GOS regression must run before acceptance.

**2026-11-01 GOS-13 integrity Gaming hard-off follow-up (SOURCE + TESTS ADDED; NOT RUN):** Replaced the uninterruptible whole-file SHA-256 helper with bounded streaming incremental hashing that checks cancellation and Gaming Mode on every read, and checked Gaming again immediately before file publication. Integrity interruption preserves the partial file, so the existing explicit resume mechanism can re-verify it later; it does not silently install or start anything. Deterministic xUnit source tests trigger Gaming after one hash chunk and cancel a pending check. Windows build, throughput, actual concurrent game-start, race/atomicity and large-file regression acceptance remain NOT RUN.

**2026-11-01 GOS-13 downloads consent/accessibility FlaUI regression (TEST SOURCE ADDED; NOT RUN):** Extended the existing Windows Games-library FlaUI smoke suite with a local loopback-only consent cancellation scenario: access to URL, destination, priority and speed controls; initial state readiness; visible confirmation before enqueue; cancelling leaves no part file or validator under the disposable test root. The existing local GOS checkpoint `-RunUi` already includes this test class; this does not assert actual HTTP transfer, restoration, WinUI compatibility or physical game hardware acceptance.

**2026-11-01 GOS-13 queue settings UI follow-up (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; BUILD/FlaUI NOT RUN):** The Games download panel now exposes low/normal/high queue priority and an explicit 0-unlimited speed cap in KiB/s backed by the existing validated Core limit endpoint. Inputs are bounded and never applied implicitly. The Core's transfer scheduler and limiter remain the existing source implementations; priority and bandwidth effects need Windows UI plus deterministic server test execution before acceptance.

**2026-11-01 GOS-13 desktop download UI (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; BUILD/FlaUI NOT RUN):** Added a first-party WinUI downloads panel within the existing Games workspace: explicit user-provided URL and destination (folder picker), optional independently obtained SHA-256 and filename, visible confirmation before enqueue, manual refresh/status/bytes, pause/resume/cancel and history removal with confirmation. It uses the existing owner-authorized Core job endpoints and cannot download automatically from a catalogue, launch an installer or run refresh polling during games. The entire panel is hidden in TV/Console mode, preserving resources for gameplay. Source still requires Windows build and FlaUI accessibility/interaction acceptance, long transfer/resume/restart physical tests, and a dedicated Downloads workspace or advanced settings if desired. Backend per-profile bandwidth UI and install hand-off remain open.

**2026-11-01 GOS-14 streaming directory scan (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; TESTS NOT RUN):** The existing game-folder scanner now uses `Directory.EnumerateFiles` / `Directory.EnumerateDirectories` rather than eagerly allocating `GetFiles` / `GetDirectories` arrays. Cancellation, 20,000 total entries, 2,000 candidate games, depth limit, no-follow-links and partial-result truncation remain enforced, including on very large source directories. This reduces startup memory/CPU spikes but needs a Windows ROM-folder stress test and an installed-emulator acceptance run.

**2026-11-01 GOS-14 bounded emulator discovery (SOURCE + SYNTHETIC TESTS; WINDOWS NOT RUN):** Emulator executable discovery now applies a global budget of 4,000 directory visits (in addition to its existing depth/cancellation rules), rather than traversing unbounded Program Files trees for each emulator. Desktop-owner discovery and game-folder scan endpoints refuse requests while persisted Gaming Mode is active, so manually scanning installed emulators or ROM folders cannot compete with foreground gameplay. Synthetic tests verify budget limits and invalid budgets. No executable, driver, game asset or firmware is downloaded/launched by this change. Real installed emulator discovery, Eden command-line behavior, gamepad mapping, configuration UI and hardware acceptance remain outstanding.

### GOS-13A — AI Game Mods during Quick Resume — PARTIAL (main source; Windows NOT RUN)
**2026-11-05 missing-mod recovery (MAIN SOURCE WIP):** An explicitly approved removal can now clear its own orphaned receipt when the installed ZIP and game EXE are both absent (including after game uninstall), without inspecting/removing any surviving game file; a dangling ZIP symlink fails closed. Present ZIPs retain mandatory EXE stop checks and SHA-before-delete. Synthetic xUnit authored-not-run; Windows path/link acceptance still pending.

**2026-11-05 game process attestation increment (MAIN SOURCE WIP):** The opt-in multi-game ZIP drop-in adapter no longer trusts the user's "game closed" checkbox alone. It now REQUIRES the actual game executable selected by the owner, validates a local regular unlinked `.exe` and directory (excluding system/security processes), conservatively probes its matching Windows process identity and fails closed for inaccessible same-name processes. It refuses install when the selected game is running/paused, records the executable in the recovery receipt, and rechecks before ZIP publication and before SHA-checked removal. Pre-existing receipts lacking the game executable stay visible as `unbound` and cannot authorize deletion, although an already-missing target can be cleared without touching game files. ### 2026-10-31 UAI-07 / Windows multi-app agent and verifiable GUI QA (SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, PARTIAL)

**2026-10-31 OS-attested cross-window GUI proof — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED; PHYSICAL WINDOWS E2E NOT RUN:** Added a bounded, **read-only** `WindowsUiTreeObservationService.CaptureVerifiedWindowAsync` for background HWND inspection, accepting only identities previously captured by Windows (not model-supplied handles) and revalidating native PID/HWND/executable path **before and after** the WinApp CLI read. It never changes focus, invokes controls, types, installs a CLI or grants desktop authority. The existing `ComputerUseGuiProofLedger` remains strict for unauthenticated/legacy single-window proofs; a new explicit `TryRecordVerifiedWindow` path can hold at most eight verified UIA control expectations across independently user-approved and OS-attested app windows. A verified `focus_window` preserves existing read-only postconditions but **resets the assertion gate** until the newly focused window is checked; any data mutation, launch, failed assertion or stale independent reread invalidates the whole ledger. Multi-window `done` requires fresh bounded UIA inspections of **every previously verified window**, each matching the recorded PID/HWND/EXE with a live allowlist check before/after, plus unchanged actual foreground and final STOP/Gaming Mode/task approval checks. Missing/replaced/ambiguous/unavailable windows, denied app approvals, changed control values and duplicate evidence cannot produce a GUI receipt ready for independent goal verification. No unapproved auxiliary/helper process inherits a parent window's proof.

This is a **sequential, non-atomic cross-application observation**, not proof of a distributed transaction, a save to durable storage, or the entire requested objective. The resulting executor receipt v1 now optionally includes bounded redacted `finalDistinctWindowsVerified` alongside `finalDistinctControlsVerified`; the Core and Agents UI validate and display these counts, preserving readable legacy v1 receipts. Pure xUnit regressions cover two independently verified process/HWND trees and rejection of stale/missing/duplicate/replaced identity evidence. The opt-in live task acceptance has an additional `-RunCrossWindowScenario`: it starts TWO disposable fixture processes with distinct synthetic nonces and HWNDs (same separately user-approved test EXE), requires real agent focus/action/assertion on BOTH, a Core-completed task-scoped approval and worker receipt with at least two final distinct windows, and independent external UIA result checks on each original process. The master Windows runner exposes a separate `-RunLiveMultiWindowE2E -CliPath <built-parux.exe>` gate with status `liveMultiWindowAgent`; neither it nor the single-window live test runs by default. This two-PID same-EXE acceptance is a first multi-process correctness test, **not** an Outlook/Excel interoperability claim. A signed native action log and physically validated third-party integrations are still open. No Windows PowerShell, .NET build, xUnit, WinApp, FlaUI or actual LLM/GUI run was executed in this environment. **UAI-07 remains PARTIAL.**



**2026-10-31 UAI-07 multiple-control exact GUI postconditions — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, NOT PHYSICALLY TESTED:** Fixed a verification correctness gap: the Computer Use worker previously stored only its **last** successful `ui_assert`, so the earlier of two controls could change asynchronously while the final verifier still admitted the task based on the second control. `ComputerUseGuiProofLedger` now collects **up to eight distinct exact controls** from proven fresh UIA assertions after the last UI-changing action, on the SAME OS-verified foreground PID/HWND. Idempotent reassertions do not consume capacity; contradictory expected values, another window/PID, invalid proofs or exceeding the cap fail closed. Any delivered UI mutation, app/window focus, unsuccessful assertion, or changed final reread clears the entire batch. At `done`, the worker obtains ONE independently fresh Windows UIA observation of the same HWND and must recheck **EVERY recorded control**, not merely the last. This is a bounded control-state guarantee, not full cross-window goal proof or certified native OS attestation; separate phases are required for different windows. The model prompt explains the 8-control bound, last-mutation semantics and no switching between assertion and `done`. Tests cover stale first/last controls, duplicates, conflicting expectations, cross-HWND/PID, capacity, redaction, and an end-to-end simulated GUI repair/receipt sequence.

The executor receipt v1 now optionally includes `finalDistinctControlsVerified` (a bounded integer, never a selector/value/title/path), which the Core preflight validates when present (`0..8`, cannot exceed passed assertions; positive for a GUI ready claim). Older v1 receipts missing this additive field remain readable. Settings → Agents can show the distinct-final-control count separately from the number of UIA assertion attempts. The opt-in `tools/test-agent-live-computer-use.ps1` now asks an actual task-scoped computer-use agent to assert BOTH the disposable fixture's `ready` input and nonce-bound `ACCEPTED-...` result after the last mutation, requires a worker receipt with at least **two distinct final verified controls**, and independently rechecks both on the original PID/HWND. Local xUnit/PowerShell runners include added tests. No real Windows execution, .NET compilation, xUnit, WinApp CLI, FlaUI or LLM-completion was performed in the current environment; **UAI-07 remains PARTIAL**.



**2026-10-31 live app-allowlist revocation and genuine modal dialogue — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, NOT WINDOWS-VALIDATED:** An app lease bound to an approved executable/PID previously continued authorizing actions even after the user removed that app from Computer Use Settings. `ComputerUseTaskAppLease.RefusalForCurrentApproval` now validates **the current allowlist** against the bound executable; it is called before GUI actions and completion, before and after a `focus_window` transition, at every model step, and via the existing STOP/Gaming Mode callback during long waits and character-by-character text typing. No revoked task app can silently reuse a same-PID modal or switch to an unapproved helper executable. A previously selected helper process similarly must retain its own executable approval. Pure xUnit regressions cover live removal/reapproval, same-PID modal windows, secondary process scope, stale screenshot/UIA after modal foreground changes, and helper modal PID mismatch.

The disposable WinForms fixture now includes a **true modal** `ShowDialog(this)` in addition to the existing modeless owned dialog. Its opening action is posted with `BeginInvoke` so WinApp's InvokePattern can return before the modal message loop blocks the parent. The dedicated opt-in `tools/test-agent-native-modal-e2e.ps1` validates one native OS-PID/owner HWND, a disabled main HWND while the modal is active, a fresh WinApp UIA Confirm on the modal, re-enabled main HWND and a separate final `MODAL-ACCEPTED-<nonce>` readback on the original main window. UAI-07 remains PARTIAL.**



**2026-10-31 Windows process-instance-scoped focus and owned dialogs — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, PHYSICAL ACCEPTANCE NOT RUN:** The task-local `ComputerUseTaskAppLease` previously compared only approved executable paths, meaning an arbitrary second process of the *same binary* could inherit the just-launched application's authority. Registered `open_app` now creates a **provisional executable-only lease with no mutation/done privilege** until an explicit `focus_window` selects one uniquely observed title and Win32 confirms the exact HWND/PID/executable before and after dispatch. The lease records the verified **PID** and denies later actions if the observed foreground process differs, even when its EXE path matches. A second window or owned popup in the same verified PID remains allowed; focusing a distinct helper process or another running copy requires an explicit `focus_window` and the target EXE to be separately approved in Computer Use Settings. No permission is inherited from an untrusted window title or a model-reported app relationship. The lease is bound inside the verified focus operation, closing the post-focus foreground-switch race. Process PID reuse/start-time attestation, signer checks, helper IPC and real app compatibility remain future work; the existing task approval and STOP/Gaming Mode still apply. Pure xUnit policy regression source covers provisional launch, same-PID dialog HWNDs, same-executable different-PID refusal, explicit rebind, denied helper and per-task isolation.

The disposable WinForms fixture now exposes `Open dialog` and `Confirm` AutomationIds. Its modeless dialog has a distinct HWND but the same process and Win32 owner as the main fixture, and confirming it updates the original window's result. `tools/test-agent-native-ui-e2e.ps1 -RunDialogScenario` is a distinct opt-in **native UIA** acceptance: discover the dialog via OS PID+GW_OWNER+exact title, read its own UIA selector, invoke Confirm, then assert the main window's changed value using a separate WinApp wait-for. **2026-10-31 opt-in LIVE PaRuX agent acceptance lane — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, NOT RUN:** Added `tools/test-agent-live-computer-use.ps1`, a separate `-RunLiveComputerUseE2E -CliPath <path-to-parux.exe>` gate in the Windows master runner, and `parux agent show <taskId> --json` (local CLI only, no change to default human output). The new lane compiles/launches one disposable WinForms fixture, captures a nonce-bound native PID/HWND and a uniquely observed Result selector, confirms the initial result is `PENDING`, asks the user to review/approve ONLY the fixture executable, then creates ONE **real** `computer-use` task through the existing Core orchestrator using the installed local agent model and the ordinary task-scoped Computer Use authorization UI. The harness does **not** approve tasks, turn on Full Computer Use, change persistent settings, run any UI mutation of its own, or query unrelated apps. The in-memory task ResultJson/model answer is never copied to the sanitized `real-agent-acceptance.json` report. **2026-10-31 deterministic GUI mismatch repair + native recovery acceptance — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, WINDOWS RUN NOT EXECUTED:** A failed exact UIA `ui_assert` previously stopped Computer Use immediately, blocking user-requested test→correct→retest cycles. A per-run `ComputerUseGuiRepairBudget` now admits **at most two** GUI-test recovery attempts for a concrete `DifferentValue`, `MissingTarget` or `MissingProperty` verdict while agent steps remain. **2026-10-31 Core-side receipt admission + Agents UI — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, NOT WINDOWS-ACCEPTED:** The desktop Settings → Agents task card now displays a bounded, schema-checked human-readable GUI receipt line: counts of attempted/delivered actions, UIA assertions and fresh final readbacks, without consuming model-provided strings, titles, selectors, paths or secret control contents. It explicitly says the run is **ready for independent goal verification**, never that the goal was passed. UAI-07 PARTIAL.**



**2026-10-31 redacted GUI task receipt / independent-verifier admission — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, TESTS NOT RUN:** A task-local `ComputerUseGuiRunTrace` now counts bounded attempted and delivered actions, actual evaluated UIA pass/fail checks, completion-time UIA readback checks and failures. The agent exposes only those event counts to the trusted desktop worker; no control values, selectors, window titles, process IDs, paths, model messages or screenshots appear in the typed receipt. The `ComputerUseGuiRunReceipt` uses a versioned fixed schema with `outcome`, `reasonCode`, `assurance`, attempted/delivered/assertion/readback counts and `readyForIndependentVerifier`. `ready` means ONLY independent-goal-verifier handoff, never a PASS certificate. The worker persists this receipt on `Verifying`, `Failed` and `Cancelled` outcomes and determines mandatory GUI checks strictly from the original user goal (not delegated/repair context or the model-reported result). A GUI test cannot reach verification unless the executor's assertion count agrees with its own recorded successful checks and a fresh final UIA readback passed. A previously failed final readback remains visible in counts even if a subsequent bounded reassertion succeeds. **2026-10-31 final GUI recheck / bounded recovery — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, NOT WINDOWS-ACCEPTED:** An earlier successful `ui_assert` is no longer sufficient at `done`: the agent now retains an ephemeral, private exact-control expectation bound to the freshly observed Windows PID/HWND. At completion it obtains a **second independent foreground UIA observation**, checks the screenshot/window provenance and user authority, and requires the **same selector/property/value** to match again. If the control changed or UIA is unavailable, the old proof is invalidated and the agent has only its existing remaining step budget to re-observe/reassert; no stale counts can request verifier approval. The worker carries a redacted `guiFinalReadbackVerified` boolean into its result and refuses the independent-verifier handoff when it is false on a GUI-test task. STOP/Gaming/approval revocation and a late foreground-window switch are also rechecked immediately before publishing any `done` completion. Expected values are never stored in logs, serialized receipts, or hashes; only in the current ephemeral process-memory proof. Added pure xUnit tests and local acceptance filter. **No .NET, FlaUI, Windows WinApp CLI, live UI or independent product acceptance was run; UAI-07 remains PARTIAL.** This checks an individual control, not the entire user goal, and is not a cryptographically signed receipt.

After a successful **registered non-built-in** `open_app`, a per-run executable lease rejects subsequent UIA/pixel actions and `done` if a fresh foreground OS identity is not the approved app. Switching to a *different explicitly registered* app through an accepted focus action can rebind the lease. **2026-10-31 follow-up hardening — SOURCE IMPLEMENTED, TESTS NOT RUN:** `open_app` now screens user-registered targets through `ComputerUseAppLaunchPolicy`: only absolute local `.exe` files; common shell/script interpreters and UNC/device paths are excluded from **autonomous** launches, without rewriting the pre-existing manually authorized fast path. This is not a complete binary/command sandbox: renamed interpreters and dangerous preconfigured arguments need a future process-attested Core permission broker. App launch now accepts the task cancellation token and rechecks Gaming before dispatch; an app already opened is not silently terminated on STOP. `ComputerUseGuiTestEvidenceGate` recognizes strong test verbs even for unfamiliar app names. Its condition is computed from the original user goal, **not** appended predecessor/web/repair text; therefore untrusted retrieved content cannot change the handoff requirement. The Worker includes redacted `guiTestProofRequired`, `guiTestProofSatisfied`, `uiAssertionsPassed` metadata, and `ComputerUseCompletionGate` refuses a GUI testing handoff without a positive assertion count and current proof. These fields are worker-reported, not cryptographically signed independent receipts. Pure regressions `ComputerUseAppLaunchPolicyTests`, `ComputerUseCompletionGateTests` and gate tests were added/expanded and selected in the local xUnit runner. All executable Windows checks remain **NOT RUN**.


**New production source on main:** Computer Use can now interpret bounded `open_app` to launch an **exact unique** built-in or user-configured application through the existing `ComputerUseService` allowlist/revalidation. The LLM sees only a bounded list of approved display names, not executable paths or arguments; unknown/ambiguous names are refused. The tool cannot launch arbitrary shell commands, install packages, browse to arbitrary URLs or grant access to apps not explicitly registered. Launch effects and subsequent window-focus changes invalidate earlier UI-test proof. Per-task Computer Use authorization, runtime Gaming hard-off, STOP, and all existing launching restrictions remain in force.

**Proof rule implemented:** when a request explicitly asks to **test/verify an app GUI**, the new `ComputerUseGuiTestEvidenceGate` refuses a model `done` claim unless a fresh exact `ui_assert` control observation has passed after the most recent UI-mutating action or window/app switch. Unsupported/missing UIA evidence cannot be silently scored as PASS. A premature `done` is fed back as a bounded, actionable next-step requirement; the agent can retry inside its existing step/time budget. The ordinary non-test task pipeline remains unchanged and is still reviewed independently. Goal-language detection is a **defensive heuristic** (English/Italian app/UI keywords), not a complete typed QA contract; it can miss cases and must be replaced with an explicit task test mode.

**Testing source:** pure `ComputerUseAppSelectionPolicyTests` and `ComputerUseGuiTestEvidenceGateTests` cover exact/ambiguous app names, refused paths/commands, test-goal handling, stale proofs, app switches and ordinary-task compatibility. Linked to `PaRuX.Server.Tests` and selected in `tools/test-agent-platform-v2.ps1` with the earlier UIA assertion/selector tests. **Tests and binaries have NOT been executed in this GitHub-only environment**: no Windows .NET SDK/WinUI, real WinApp CLI or FlaUI available. This is neither independently reviewed nor physically release-accepted.

**Critical functionality not yet delivered:** (a) discovery of arbitrary installed Windows apps and a user-approved "add to app allowlist" workflow; (b) reliable app/window lifecycle identity for launches, multiwindow dialogs, restart and cleanup; (c) semantic mapping of screenshot pixel clicks/typing to independently observed controls or a safe per-action approval broker — screenshot-only unlabeled activation is intentionally denied, so it must NOT be advertised as usable arbitrary-app control; (d) native GUI assertions verified by real FlaUI/WinApp against Notepad + a disposable WinUI test app; (e) an explicit typed app-testing task mode and immutable run receipt, independent goal verifier, test execution/recovery and model-guided bounded repair; (f) sanctioned Outlook/Teams/Excel and browser test workflows with test accounts; (g) authenticated Graph/Google connector work UAI-08…UAI-12. **These are required SOFTWARE + PHYSICAL ACCEPTANCE tasks.**

**Next integration work in order:** test and fix this exact source on Windows → add app inventory/consent UX and task-local window process lease → finish semantic UIA and read-only assertions with multi-window/foreground-change evidence → build a disposable end-to-end "open test app → edit value → assert exact result → independent verifier" task → implement Core-owned connector capability/approval broker and read-only Microsoft/Google adapters. Never assume "all Windows applications supported" from a generic action grammar.



### 2026-10-30 Computer Use app-testing slice — IMPLEMENTED IN SOURCE, NOT ACCEPTED

Windows Computer Use now supports a typed read-only `ui_assert` action, comparing exactly one WinApp UIA control's `name`, `value` or `text` with a bounded expected string in a **fresh** observation of the same foreground HWND/PID. Missing selectors, mismatched properties, duplicated selectors, another window, changed focus and unavailable fresh evidence do **not** pass. Expected/observed values are not included in action labels or task logs. UI assertions refuse password/OTP or unlabelled controls rather than becoming a credential oracle. UIA action selectors and the sensitivity-policy label resolver now require the observed HWND, rather than accepting an identically named control in a second window. Open-app `focus_window` uses unique/exact-title selection, refuses ambiguous matches instead of focusing the first window, and redacts document-bearing titles from logs. This is a small but real foundation for testing arbitrary already-open Windows applications via UI Automation when explicitly authorized, not comprehensive app control.

New test coverage includes independent assertion policy, schema/grammar, wrong-window spoofing, duplicates, malformed UIA responses, bound expected values and sensitivity labels; these tests are **SOURCE ONLY, NOT RUN** in this environment. A fresh Windows WinUI build, targeted xUnit, real WinApp CLI, FlaUI and a disposable multi-app GUI test are required. Full cross-app parity remains OPEN: enumerate/launch **user-authorized** apps and track child windows, handle modal dialogs and multiple monitors, preserve per-app ownership and permissions, verify goals via independent UIA/DOM or app-native readback, support bounded self-repair after failed checks, and give the user preview/STOP/pause/approval cards. Distinguish test failures from agent/model assertions and forbid automatic privileged/UAC/DLP/MFA bypass. Source and physical acceptance remain PARTIAL.


**User goal / priority decision:** transform PaRuX into a genuinely useful local-first assistant with the simplicity of Codex/Claude Code, the local model/media breadth of Locally Uncensored, and competitive training/performance comparable to relevant Unsloth workflows. The user should describe a goal once in Chat and receive a verified outcome; PaRuX should autonomously choose permitted models, skills, tools, browser or computer interfaces and connected applications. **This is an implementation target, not a best-in-class claim.**

**Single plan rule:** THIS file remains the sole operational backlog and status authority. [Universal Agent / App Connectors architecture reference](docs/UNIVERSAL_AGENT_APP_CONNECTORS_ARCHITECTURE.md) details interface design, permissions, provider limitations, 18 executable scenario definitions and security/test acceptance; it is **not** a parallel plan. [Agent Platform V2 evidence](docs/AGENT_PLATFORM_V2.md) retains prior technical work and unverified experiment status. Reuse existing BIC-01–BIC-17 rather than resetting passed gates or counting the same capability twice.

**Contemporary competitive baselines (official sources checked 2026-10-30):**
- **Codex**: coordinated parallel agents/worktrees, inspectable diffs, browser and computer-use workflows, skill/plugin extensibility and recurring work are relevant *product workflow* targets, not claims of on-device model throughput. See https://openai.com/index/introducing-the-codex-app/ and https://openai.com/index/codex-for-almost-everything/ .
- **Locally Uncensored**: v3.0.7 (2026-10-19) includes cross-Create trained characters, ComfyUI workflow imports with dependency nodes, multi-image reference editing, personas and chat branching; future v3.0.8 roadmap items are NOT treated as already shipped. See https://github.com/PurpleDoubleD/locally-uncensored/releases .
- **Unsloth Desktop/Studio**: its current public scope is broader than training alone: local inference, no-code fine-tuning, dataset preparation, agent-client connections, web research and image/video creation. Compare relevant shared *workflows* as well as training performance, subject to hardware. See https://www.unsloth.ai/ and https://unsloth.ai/docs .
- **Claude Code**: coding/task orchestration, tool granularity, terminal/edit/review, safe permission design and ease of use are references, but comparison of cloud model intelligence to CPU/Vulkan local models must disclose model and cost differences. Verify each competitor's current actual release before claiming parity.

**Source evidence checked on public main 2026-10-30:** `DesktopIntegrationCatalog/Service/Tools` handle opt-in read-only local folders: .eml/mbox, .ics, Office files, bookmarks, markdown/Obsidian, synced OneDrive directories. `DocumentWriteTools/OfficeDocumentWriter` generate/edit copies of local DOCX/XLSX. `CodingAgentService`, Chat, web search, browser CDP, UIA/vision computer use, runtime routing, Model Hub, media/training, Universal Gateway and existing agent foundations are substantive. However, **live authenticated Microsoft Graph Outlook/Teams/OneDrive/SharePoint/Excel and Google Gmail/Calendar/Drive/Sheets connectors are NOT implemented** by these local readers. User consent, app-specific scopes, actual send/invite/update, conflict-safe provider mutations and account isolation therefore need new software. OpenCode observation is **not** an authorized executing backend. Browser-wide OS egress confinement and full chat-to-verified UI acceptance are outstanding. Previous desktop hardware/UX changes still require executable acceptance.

**Public/repository constraints:** no GitHub Actions, no real user documents/accounts/tokens/sample messages, no corporate tenant or shared credentials in Git, no unapproved Microsoft/Google app registrations. No silent USER_MANAGED changes, cloud inference, network grants, agent sends/deletes, Git pushes or app installs. FSL-1.1-MIT is source-available, not OSI open-source; review licenses before reuse. Windows 10/11 x64 desktop remains target; CPU-only, AMD/Intel integrated/discrete, NVIDIA and eGPU compatibility must be capability-detected, never inferred from one test workstation. Company and Google Workspace admin policies may require IT approval; report `BLOCKED-EXTERNAL` when an actual provider permission is denied, not "fixed" by credential scraping or RPA circumvention.

### Product contract — one task, one coherent user experience

1. **One Chat home** with attach/drag-drop/voice, intelligent optional online/PC tools, context-specific app connection CTA; automatic local model selected by verifiable capability, quality and latency, without jargon. Advanced can pin exact model/quantization/backend/context and inspect every grant, retry, step and benchmark.
2. **One durable orchestrator**: user goal → explicit bounded plan → agents/tools and exact identities → preview and least-privilege approvals → execution → independent postcondition verifier → deliverables/citations/diffs → resume, repair or STOP. Chat shows concise milestones and current next action; details disclose evidence/tool logs on request. An API/tool returning success is not proof of the user's goal.
3. **One permission broker** controls local files, shell/worktrees, browser domains, Windows foreground app, individual provider account/tenant and remote data. Tool output, email, webpage content, MCP and model messages are *untrusted data*, never a grant. Read, draft, reversible write, external send/share and destructive action are separate risk bands. Exact recipient/target/payload/expiration/identity requires fresh confirmation for external effects.
4. **One connection center** with clear account choice, capabilities and truthful statuses: unsupported, not connected, consent required, admin blocked, read-only, write-with-approval, temporarily offline, expired or revoked. Local file export/sync is labelled "local snapshot", not authenticated Outlook/Gmail. Offline Chat continues with available models/files and never claims a remote app was updated.
5. **One verifiable result**: evidence links and retrieval freshness, provider-issued IDs/version/ETag for remote commits, independently observed UI/DOM or build/test for computer/coding tasks, explicit partial and uncertain-outcome states, safe retry/rollback when possible. No duplicate emails on timeout or interrupted tasks.
6. **One hardware-aware scheduler** across text, research, coding, web, UI, media and training: task-quality and first-useful-response targets, bounded RAM/VRAM, residency, parallel reads vs serialized conflicting writes, queue visibility, STOP, Gaming hard-off, power/resource pressure. Local, browser/API and optional paid-cloud egress/cost are displayed separately.

**Delivery dependency graph:**
- `UAI-00 → (UAI-01 + UAI-03) → UAI-04` is the FIRST shippable task-flow slice; `UAI-02` supplies its novice UI.
- `UAI-05/06/07` extend the *same* coordinator and verifier, not separate incompatible task engines; a read-only web tool or UIA inspector is not execution parity.
- `UAI-08 → UAI-09/10 (read) → UAI-11 (approved write) → UAI-12 (cross-app)`; each must remain optional, not gate local first-run.
- `UAI-13/14/15` share benchmark/resource and evidence services; `UAI-16/17/18` make features reliable and competitive. `UAI-19/20` reuse Core app grants and task state.
- Parallel work permitted only for separable adapter/test fixtures with one owner each; no independent conflicting edits of Chat orchestration, authorization, schemas or global Settings layout.

### Integration coverage and honest access routes

| Family | Desired real workflows | Initial route / permissions | Fallback when unavailable |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Microsoft Outlook email + calendar | Search/read with citations, thread summary, drafts, reviewed send, meetings/invites | Graph delegated account/tenant scopes with sanctioned Entra consent | Existing exported .eml/.ics read-only; manually opened approved draft. No promise of send/booking |
| Microsoft Teams | Permitted chat/channel research, draft/send, file links, action extraction | Graph delegated work/school account; separate chat/channel scopes; do not use Teams as a log sink | User-initiated open-in-app and approved UIA only if policy allows; read-only unavailable if no approved route |
| OneDrive + SharePoint | Search selected files, versions, citations, approved upload and update | Graph identity/scoped files/sites; verify placeholder vs synced content | Explicitly connected local synchronized folder; label snapshot/offline; never infer tenant access |
| Excel / Word / PowerPoint | Existing local DOCX/XLSX creation/copy-edit and DOCX/PPTX read; additional PPTX creation remains software work. Online Excel business-workbook ranges/tables are future API writes | Existing OpenXML first; Graph Excel `.xlsx` workbook API only for supported OneDrive for Business/SharePoint locations (not OneDrive Consumer); approved copy/upload for other file scopes | Save a reviewable local copy rather than silently modifying a coauthored online workbook; validate Word/PPTX fidelity independently |
| Gmail + Google Calendar | Message/thread search, reply drafts, approved send and calendar events | Google OAuth with minimal scopes; restricted Gmail scopes may require provider verification/security assessment | Explicit .eml/.ics export or manually approved UI workflow, never browser cookie scraping |
| Google Drive / Sheets / Docs / Slides | Scoped search, spreadsheet update, versioned copy/upload | Google delegated OAuth; prefer `drive.file` where technically suitable | Local selected export/editable copies, no impersonation of live cloud sync |
| Other local or web software | Knowledge, files, creative tools, productivity, IDEs and repeatable user tasks | Official API → typed MCP/Skills with Core policy → UIA/DOM with explicit user consent | Explain unsupported and safe manual handoff; never bypass MFA, enterprise DLP or app restrictions |

**External rules that materially affect feasibility:** Microsoft Entra tenant user-consent policy may block even delegated `Mail.Read`/Teams/SharePoint access; administrators cannot be bypassed. Graph file access to personal OneDrive is not equivalent to the Excel Workbook REST API, which currently does not support OneDrive Consumer. Teams `ChatMessage.Send` is delegated for work/school identities; read/write permissions and message types differ. Graph Excel APIs are not a universal substitute for desktop Office. Gmail read/modify scopes can be restricted and require Google OAuth verification and possibly a security assessment for general-public use. Google Drive per-file `drive.file` is a narrower option where feasible. Corporate source data cannot be sent to an optional cloud model without distinct provider/data egress consent. See checked primary API references in the architecture document. Treat external tenant registration/approval, provider verification and real test accounts as *real blockers for live use*, not blockers for implementing hermetic transports and local workflows.

### Action policy / data protection requirements (hard gates)

- **Read path:** user chooses an account and accessible collection; sources carry provider ID, item ID, tenant/account, timestamp, freshness and citations. Limit traversal and attachments; no automatic full-mailbox indexing. Responses and cached content never become a model grant or override a developer policy.
- **Write path:** model proposes a typed effect, Core independently computes target/version/diff/recipient/attachment digest and displays it. Fresh approval is checked again immediately before sending or saving. Permissions only shrink on child delegation; revocation cancels outstanding work. Every side effect has provider ID/readback or truthful `result_unknown`; blind retry of send is prohibited.
- **Network:** no cookie/token scraping, disabled internet and optional remote model stay distinct; browser child targets, SSRF/DNS/redirect, download, filesystem, Clipboard and connected tools must obey the same Core-owned policy. Browser-automation fallback never circumvents tenant restrictions. Tokens encrypted, redacted, revocable and absent from prompts/public artifacts.
- **Data:** strict account/tenant isolation, opt-in indexing, delete/tombstone on revocation, durable consent evidence, privacy-aware export and deletion. Source file and cloud revision collisions produce a reviewable conflict; original data preserved. Multilingual output must never conceal scope/approval in translation.
- **Reliability:** bounded timeouts, retries and jitter, provider `Retry-After`, observable 401/403/409/412/429, idempotent mutation receipts where available, no duplicate sending; source current-state checks after restart. STOP/Gaming hard-off apply to agents and owned workers, with honest warning if a remote side effect has already committed.

### Outcome-oriented, cross-product acceptance (test before calling "better")

**Golden paths (must exist as automated integration + physical acceptance as applicable):** novice offline workbook creation; attach source → verified report; real repo coding with tests and GUI assertion; web research with citations and no unauthorized egress; one authorized Windows UI task with readback; Outlook/Teams source-grounded summary; Gmail draft/send-to-self; calendar conflict-safe invitation; provider Excel/Sheets targeted update; expired login/reconnect; user interrupts cross-app execution; user denies permission; enterprise tenant blocks consent; native GPU unavailable and CPU fallback; browser/mail prompt-injection attack denied. Exact 18 scenarios and metadata contracts are in the architecture reference.

**Non-negotiable security acceptance:** zero unauthorized send/share/delete; no cross-account data, no MFA/DLP bypass, no secret/PII leaked to external inference or public logs; canceled or `result_unknown` step never appears complete. Mock success cannot be labelled physical provider acceptance.

**Performance/quality:** representative task completion correctness first; measure first *useful* response, warm vs cold load, solved work per minute, human interventions, p50/p95 end-to-end latency, retries/errors, memory/power and connector quota. A proposed >=90% correct held-out mixed-task success threshold and practical sub-second UI responsiveness are targets only, not achieved metrics. Benchmark same file hash/model/quantization/context/hardware and a fair workflow; Codex/Claude cloud outcomes should be compared separately from on-device tok/s. Record failures and statistical uncertainty, not only favorable runs.

**DoD / gate commands:** implement coherent vertical slices with source/contract tests, hermetic Graph/Google responses, Windows native + FlaUI/real CDP tests, authorized external sandbox provider smoke, independent review and unchanged private/public data boundaries. Run repo-prescribed local validation `tools/validate-software-complete.ps1 -IncludeAndroid` and applicable release gates only when a suitable Windows machine is available. Maintain `PARTIAL`/`MISSING` until proven; use `BLOCKED-EXTERNAL` on a confirmed unavailable API/tenant/provider approval. Do not invent execution of .NET, WinUI, browser or any third-party account in a GitHub-only documentation turn.

**NEXT IMPLEMENTATION TASK — UAI-00 then UAI-01/03/04:** inspect current production Chat/agent/desktop-file/document service and their tests; define minimal Task + Capability + Evidence + Approval contracts that do not duplicate existing policy stores; wire a small real **local-only** workbook/document task through Chat with no mandatory model reinstall or network; test copy-preservation, completion verifier, consent, task resume and STOP. Land source + tests in small commits, then separately prioritize UAI-08 and read-only Microsoft/Google adapters. Do **not** start with unrestricted enterprise write APIs, broad mailbox ingest, or a redesign that abandons working PaRuX workspaces.

### 2026-10-30 desktop GPU/Core reconciliation (PARTIAL — Windows acceptance outstanding)

The read-only Settings hardware monitor now obtains Core's normalized adapter and installed-backend eligibility, rather than treating NVIDIA VRAM or the largest DXGI adapter as evidence that local inference can use it. AMD/Intel UMA reports shared RAM separately from dedicated VRAM. A pure mapper guards cross-adapter telemetry attribution and still distinguishes an externally measured GPU from a verified executable backend when Core is offline; Settings model fit fails conservatively to CPU/RAM or Unknown on a failed Core probe. Core inventory is refreshed on a short TTL for eGPU changes; vendor telemetry and Core status are sampled concurrently with bounded timeouts and failed-probe backoff. Focused deterministic unit tests were added for mixed/eGPU, Intel/AMD UMA, unsupported runtimes, missing Core and Windows software adapters; **none has been executed**. WinUI builds, xUnit, FlaUI, real unplug/replug and inference on non-NVIDIA hardware remain pending. This is not a release acceptance claim.

### 2026-10-30 cross-PC compatibility continuation (PARTIAL — physical acceptance outstanding)

The public `main` branch now treats CPU-only Windows x64 PCs as first-class model-fit candidates when measured system RAM exists, instead of requiring GPU VRAM to produce a verdict. The Settings fit label distinguishes CPU/RAM-only compatibility from GPU offload and warns of reduced speed. No GPU memory is fabricated when DXGI or vendor telemetry is unavailable. Model-fit previews with explicit context but no parsed GGUF header take the marked size-heuristic path. The desktop fit estimator and Core hardware profile resolver both use a conservative half-RAM budget through 16 GiB, retaining the older 8 GiB/25% reserve for larger PCs; live admission remains independently constrained by current free-memory evidence. Narrow/high-DPI Gallery cards now stack their controls, and Model Hub's quantization dialog no longer requires a 330px-wide picker.

Regression tests were added/extended for CPU-only 8/16/32 GiB, 4–64 GiB reserve profiles, Intel UMA with 8 GiB RAM, AMD UMA, NVIDIA/dedicated and unknown/missing-inventory behavior; these are source changes **NOT EXECUTED** in this environment. Before declaring broad hardware compatibility, execute the .NET tests, WinUI build, FlaUI, real local text and media operations, live memory-pressure and device disconnect/reconnect checks on the concrete Windows machines listed in `docs/validation/PHYSICAL_PC_ACCEPTANCE.md`. Linux/macOS/x86 are not supported targets; unsupported model backends must still report unavailable rather than silently claiming support. BIC/release acceptance unchanged.

### 2026-10-30 UI/UX continuation (PARTIAL — Windows validation pending)

On main, progressive disclosure now covers Model Hub, Chat manual model labels, Model Routing, Tools/Automations, Media Gallery, Runtime suggestions and context controls, Observability, Updates and Projects. This continuation adds a responsive Settings category selector (with the desktop rail preserved), per-category on-demand Settings Core refresh in place of eager cross-category fan-out, compact Create media model labels with an exact-identity Details dialog and an optional global media lane editor, and concise first-run Onboarding with collapsible runtime and storage details. Existing Core mutation endpoints, model pin identity, runtime ownership restrictions and approval boundaries were not intentionally altered.

Static source review and XAML tag-balance checks were performed; source-level xUnit contract tests were added but **NOT RUN**. WinUI compilation, real responsive/window-size behavior, keyboard/screen-reader paths, FlaUI regression and physical Windows/GPU acceptance are **NOT RUN**. No release gate, benchmark improvement, or BIC acceptance row is claimed. Follow up with local Windows build, relevant xUnit filters, FlaUI Settings/Create/Onboarding/Model Hub tests and a clean-install manual pass; fix any failures before classifying UX work as DONE.



**Agent Platform V2 implementation (isolated branch, NOT release-accepted):** [docs/AGENT_PLATFORM_V2.md](docs/AGENT_PLATFORM_V2.md) specifies a dependency-gated integration track for context-surviving local coding agents, optional OpenCode execution, Windows UIA/visual Computer Use, Internet/browser work and real UI verification. The isolated branch now includes final-prompt exact-token admission, checkpoint continuation and write-ahead dispatch recovery, local search, guarded CDP browser/UIA integration, read-only OpenCode observation, browser UI evidence redaction, ephemeral task-owned browser profiles, same-session coding-run exclusion, typed DOM postcondition assertions, strict local-only UI mutation authority, verified-process-exit browser shutdown, corrected typed CDP retry/denial handling, filtered literal private/metadata IP targets even in Internet ON mode, and hardened coding-task resume so answered questions or delegated pauses cannot waive pending build/test verification (including legacy checkpoint repair). Core now independently rejects malformed/pending coding verification metadata, binds claimed successful commands to the task checkpoint, and requires actual task-owned stored PNG/SHA-256 screenshot evidence with stable foreground-window and UIA provenance before an UI-verification task can reach the verifier model. New focused tests and SQLite-backed integration cases are wired but have NOT run on Windows. The current draft additionally includes a disposable WinForms UIA E2E fixture and optional local runner, nonce-scoped localhost browser fixture with Node contract tests, strict WinApp JSON assertion checks, invalidation of all previously satisfied coding verification scopes after every testable edit, retained task checkpoints until independent review, fresh Core-approved apply gating, same-review diff fingerprints for single/multi-repository adoption, and stable UI AutomationIds in coding controls. Coding command success is now bound to a complete Git-state fingerprint saved in the durable checkpoint, rechecked at done and again by the desktop worker before Core handoff; the task result carries this fingerprint and Core/adoption gates reject malformed or absent digests. Indirect file edits after a successful build/test invalidate the old evidence, with the narrow trust boundary of worker-reported command results explicitly documented. Coding task execution also now holds a SHA-256 keyed exclusive OS file lease for the entire RunAsync scope; this prevents two PaRuX processes sharing the same state root from interleaving one checkpoint, while cross-host Core fencing with distinct state roots remains open. None of these constitutes physical Windows acceptance, and the source still has substantive unresolved software gaps. The shadow-to-original project copy now treats missing Git status, failed intent-to-add and unverifiable destination/source hashes as hard failures, verifies both paths of renames before overwriting/deleting, and reports missing source/deletion/baseline-commit failures rather than falsely marking them applied. Added conflict regressions and symlink-safe mirror traversal. Crash-safe rollback for a partially applied non-Git folder remains an explicit software gap, not merely a PC test. A typed unresolved-journal result now opens a recovery dialog (restore originals, accept an all-applied state behind a second irreversible-deletion confirmation, or inspect a conflicting or invalid journal) backed by the locked recovery service, and a failed journal check no longer leaks the project lease; this is unit-tested only (Shadow filter 86/86 passing) and has NOT been exercised by a real crash, power loss or the launched UI. Approved coding changes now carry the exact HEAD-bound Git workspace fingerprint from the chat confirmation into the single-, multi- and shadow-repository apply services. Each service rejects changed source both on entry and after preparing temporary intent-to-add patches; shadow copies also reject traversal/device/symlink paths, and the recursive shadow mirror no longer follows reparse-point subtrees. Real Git positive/negative adoption regressions and shadow path tests are selected in the local Windows runner but are NOT RUN here. Multi-process races after the last proof and partial shadow-folder write rollback remain genuine software tasks. Worktree provenance now distinguishes a trust check from actual Git presence: a single repository must have a real .git marker, and aggregate workspaces must contain 2..16 validated direct Git authorities. A prior green verification receipt now binds the HEAD commit as well as tracked/untracked changes in each alias, so committing after testing cannot silently retain an old PASS. Windows-safe task/alias validation is consistent across multi-worktree creation, review/apply and verification discovery; traversal, reserved device names and duplicate case-insensitive aliases are blocked before filesystem creation. Genuine temporary Git and actual multi-worktree regression tests are wired but NOT RUN on Windows. The verified-source gate is now stricter: all authorized Git aliases must participate in a composite `verify-all` command, with safe alias names, no unsupported third repository, and no invented aggregate-root test; an accepted discovered command requires an unchanged, Git-tracked root verifier manifest before and after execution so a rewritten npm/Make/build script cannot produce a false green. Browser completion checks now require an exact nonblank `text_equals` or `value_equals` assertion after a mutation; trivial `exists(body)` no longer clears the durable postcondition. Genuine Windows/.NET and browser acceptance remain unexecuted. Further changes require an explicit coding `done` claim before verifier handoff; retain a durable mandatory `browser_assert` postcondition after browser click/type/eval, check that state again in Core and before user adoption, and attach new child CDP targets paused with fail-closed browser termination on unguarded secondary contexts. An opt-in Node 22 + Chromium browser/CDP loopback fixture was added to the local runner but has not passed real target-PC acceptance. It still lacks an OS-confined executing OpenCode adapter, browser-wide/OS-level DNS and egress confinement for unrelated browser processes, and a verified coding → build → real native UI assertion → independent acceptance flow. Windows build, xUnit, CDP/UIA, GPU and real-device acceptance for this branch remain **NOT RUN**. Existing BIC statuses and release gates are unchanged. Do not mark this experimental branch as a completed or shipped capability.

This is the **one active operational roadmap** for PaRuX. Retained dated evidence describes earlier work; old status words are evidence only and must never be interpreted as current blockers. The public-history cleanup intentionally removed archived plans and old commit history. Historical commit identifiers in dated evidence are contextual labels, not resolvable references in the sanitized repository.

All existing BIC/release and hardware gaps remain OPEN. This UI work must not be described as complete release acceptance.

**Item 1 — DONE (real UI run passed on 2026-10-26).** Three defects were found and fixed test-first: (1) `CodeWorktreeApplyService` decoded `git diff` stdout with the console code page and re-encoded it, corrupting non-ASCII hunk context so `git apply --check` failed at `CodingAgentArtifactStore.cs:156` (commit `1cc8dc2`: Git writes the patch via `--output`; a second preflight with line-ending normalization handles LF patches on CRLF targets; real conflicts still refused); (2) the review list parsed abbreviated paths (`.../File.cs`) from `git diff --stat`, which as a pathspec matched nothing, so one of two reviewed files was silently skipped while apply reported success (commit `d68da4f`: unabbreviated review stat, apply refuses with "Nothing was applied" when any accepted entry is not a real changed path, Git output decoded as UTF-8); (3) stops were labelled generically and context shortfalls simply failed (commit `5896e4b`: specific stop reasons — context too small / repeated action / no progress / sandbox — shown as the task stage and error with a next step, and before failing on context the agent raises the coding model window once via the Runtime Manager planner, reloads, and retries the step; pure policy unit-tested 11/11, App build 0 errors; the live auto-raise path itself was NOT exercised end to end). Evidence: `CodeWorktreeApplyServiceTests` + `AcceptedFileSelectionApplyTests` + `AgentWorktreeGitTrustTests` 26/26 (new regressions were red first). Real FlaUI/UIA test `Public_clone_artifact_search_recovers_regex_alternatives_and_applies_through_real_chat` on a freshly published app (Qwen3.8-27B UD-IQ4_XS on CUDA 13.4, 16384 context with q8_0 KV cache): PASS 1/1 in 7m50s, task "Completed and verified" unaided, verifier PASS, Apply via the real button and confirmation dialog, `success=True`, both reviewed files applied, applied file contents identical to the reviewed worktree, workspace HEAD unchanged, no manual restart or OK-press. Limits: 11 attempts were needed in total — 5 failed because of my isolated-environment setup (missing context preference gave an 8192-token window), 3 because the 27B model looped on identical reads and the guard stopped it, 1 exposed defect (2); the agent is not yet reliable on this model/hardware (it passed 2 of the last 4 attempts with the corrected setup). Context headroom on a 16 GB card is VRAM-dependent (14 GB model); the 8192-token pause appears when free VRAM is short. `MultiRepositoryApplyService` and `ShadowWorkspaceService` use the same stat/pathspec pattern but were not changed or exercised. The 1.9.7 MSI built earlier (source `b1f7ac3`) contains fix (1) but NOT (2)/(3): rebuild before any publication.

(2) Chat in English ("Can you create an image of a lighthouse at night during a storm, dramatic waves?") and Italian ("Puoi creare un\'immagine di un gatto che dorme su un divano rosso, luce calda?") each produced a real image inline in Chat. (3) Create > Video > "Set up video generation" (Wan 2.2 TI2V 5B fp16 10 GB, umt5 text encoder, VAE, all pinned) then Create produced a real H.264 1280x720, 5 s, 24 fps MP4 (about 6 min cold, 30 steps) and Chat in Italian ("Fammi un video di un faro sotto la tempesta…") produced a second real clip with an inline player. This first failed with "I need an attached image to edit" because the latest assistant message was an unrelated error turn; the latest generated image is now found past error and text-only turns (`LastGeneratedImageTests`). (5) Create > Speech > "Set up speech": the first attempt failed because `uv python install` aborts with "Missing expected target directory for Python minor version link" under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\PaRuX` (it works in Temp or Documents; junctions can be created there manually), so speech could not be installed on this PC at all; PaRuX now skips uv when the interpreter exists and falls back to a staging install it adopts (`ManagedPythonMinorLinkFallbackTests`); after that the setup completed in about 4 min, Create > Speech synthesized a real 16.7 s WAV and `/api/v1/speech/transcribe` returned "Hello. This is Pa Ru X speaking from your own computer." for it (STT first call 41 s including model load; the Transcribe button itself was not driven because it opens the native file picker). (6) Create > 3D > "Set up image to 3D", "Make 3D" from the last result, "Generate 3D model" produced an 11.9 MB `.glb` (valid glTF 2.0 header, one mesh) in under a minute. Subjective media quality is not graded (owner decision). Independent code review of the setup service (read-only agent) found eight issues; the seven real ones were fixed with tests (companion "all clear" from an absent size, cancel/timeout not stopping jobs, cancel/start ignoring the media type, truncated copy treated as installed, checkpoint without SHA-256 pin, stuck fresh-model queue entry, misleading timeout text); the prior-image heuristic gap is mitigated by the "ask about image" rule. (7) Music (new capability: the catalogue listed ACE-Step as resolver-only with no engine): `MusicWorkflow` (ACE-Step 1.5 turbo all-in-one graph validated by hand against ComfyUI v0.42.1: 30 s stereo 48 kHz FLAC in 26 s), `MusicService` + `POST /api/v1/music/generate` (exclusive GPU lease, managed media runtime, debounced text-model restore, ComfyUI output path confined to its output folder), a pinned 9.34 GB checkpoint setup step (Hugging Face commit `6707deb2…`, SHA-256 `67b0f43a…` verified by the download service, Apache-2.0) and a Create > Music tab (style, lyrics, length, language, audio player, artifact capture) plus a Chat music route in English and Italian ("Compose a song about the sea at night", "Componi una canzone sul mare di notte"; "how do I compose a song in Ableton", "music video", "lyrics of a song" do not trigger it). For song requests the local assistant writes the lyrics in the requested language. Verified through the real UI: Create > Music > "Set up music" downloaded the checkpoint and Create produced a 30 s FLAC whose speech-to-text transcript was "Morning days on no show. Coffee war when the word is still sing it slow, sing it free. This little song is made for me." (the lyrics entered); Chat "Compose a song about the sea at night, calm and dreamy" returned a 45 s song with an inline audio player whose transcript contained assistant-written lyrics about the sea ("Silent Waves against the reef. Moonlight touches gentle crest…"), and the Italian request returned Italian lyrics (detected language "it": "Il mare canta solo e silenzioso… le onde dormono sotto il cielo d'argento"). (8) Character cards: Settings > Personas > "Import character card…" (SillyTavern/Chub v2/v3 JSON, v1 flat JSON, or PNG with an embedded `chara`/`ccv3` chunk; creator notes are not turned into instructions) creates a persona; verified by importing a v2 card, activating it and getting an in-character Chat answer ("Ahoy, landlubber. The name's Captain Mira…"); the file-picker click itself was not driven (the API route was). NOT run: Android/web media tabs, QR scanning, running the media paths from the signed MSI build, Chat video from English phrasing.

**Background removal (new, Item 3 — PARTIAL: engine proven for real, UI/Chat path built but not driven in the real App).** `CutoutService` (managed venv under `ai-runtimes\cutout-studio`, onnxruntime 1.31.1 CPU + Pillow 13.0.0 + NumPy 2.4.6, no system Python) runs BiRefNet-lite ONNX (`onnx-community/BiRefNet_lite-ONNX` at commit `de15b22b…`, 224,005,088 bytes, SHA-256 `56000243…3333`, MIT per both model cards, not gated), downloaded through a size- and SHA-256-checked HTTPS downloader and re-verified before use. `GET /api/v1/cutout/engine`, `POST /api/v1/cutout/engine/install` and `POST /api/v1/cutout/remove` (base64 PNG/JPEG/WebP, 25 MB and 36 MP caps read from the header, magic bytes not file name, work files confined to the cutout work folder, Gaming Mode cancels, desktop-owner only; not exposed to paired devices) return a transparent PNG as a `MediaAsset`, refusing a result without an alpha channel or with no foreground. Setup type `cutout` (engine + model steps, licences shown), Create > Edit "Remove background" plus result-strip button, and Chat: "remove the background / rimuovi lo sfondo / togli lo sfondo / ritaglia il soggetto / make the background transparent" on an attached or last generated image runs the cutout; "replace/change the background", colours, "and then…" and how-to questions stay with the diffusion edit or chat (`CutoutPhrasingTests`, `CutoutEngineTests`). Real run (clean test data root, CPU): install + model download + verify + cutout in 36 s total; 1024x1024 fox photo returned an RGBA PNG (1.33 MB, 75.5% of pixels transparent, 22.4% opaque, 2.1% soft edge), 15.6 s per 1024x1024 image on the CPU. Open: App UI and Chat click-through, large-image timing, GPU/DirectML provider (deliberately CPU-only).

**More media families (2026-10-27 — PARTIAL: Qwen-Image and Qwen-Image-Edit are now supported and downloadable with graphs proven well-formed against a real ComfyUI 0.42.1; NOT yet run on the RTX 5080; LTX-Video remains resolver-only).** `qwen-image` is a supported companion-registry family for the `image` and `edit` pipelines with two templates (`qwen-image-api.json`, `qwen-image-edit-api.json`, new optional registry key `pipelineTemplates`), signature stems read from the real 1933-tensor header of `qwen_image_fp8_e4m3fn.safetensors` by HTTP Range (header only), and pinned companions (`qwen_2.5_vl_7b_fp8_scaled` 9,384,670,680 B and `qwen_image_vae` 253,806,246 B, commit `1f12b17b`, SHA-256 and licence from the repository). Catalogue: `qwen-image-fp8` and `qwen-image-edit-fp8` (20,430,635,136 B each, exact listing, commit and SHA-256 pinned, tier 5). A checkpoint of another family now runs that family's own template (`MediaCheckpointResolution.TemplatePathFor`), never the default graph with a foreign file patched in; the edit runner patches the instruction/seed input names each graph really has. Honest limits: Qwen-Image and Qwen-Image-Edit weights have identical tensor names, so a header cannot tell them apart and the edit gate trusts which file the user assigns to editing; `qwen_image_2512_*` files carry a `model.diffusion_model.` prefix the detector refuses, so they are not offered; the GGUF build stays listed-only; image-to-image still has only the Z-Image graph. Proof so far: structural tests against a snapshot of the real node list, throw-away CPU ComfyUI validation (only the missing model files were reported), no GPU job. Owner to verify on the RTX 5080: download Qwen-Image (fp8) in Model Hub, run Create > Image, then Qwen-Image-Edit for Create > Edit; time it, since 20 GB of weights offload to system RAM on 16 GB. LTX-Video 2B v0.9.5 was verified against its listing (6,340,729,500 B) but needs a full-checkpoint loader and its own video runner contract, so it is not offered.

**Item 4 (performance) — DONE for text decode/prefill on this PC (2026-10-27, RTX 5080 16 GB, quiet machine, `tools/perf-launch-bench.py`, same GGUF files, same prompts, temperature 0, 3 warm runs, medians; raw JSON in the git-ignored `.ai/artifacts/session-20261026/perf/`).** Engines: PaRuX CUDA 13.4 b11912, LM Studio 2.41.0 CUDA12 llama.cpp backend (its own vendor DLLs), Locally Uncensored `lu-llama-server.exe`. Identical flags (`--ctx-size 8192 --batch-size 3072 --ubatch-size 768 -ctk/-ctv q8_0 -fa on -ngl 99 --parallel 1`). Qwen3.5-4B Q4_K_M: decode PaRuX 178.7 tok/s vs LM Studio 167.6 (+6.6%) vs LU 135.7 (+32%); 2k-token prefill 8161 vs 6794 (+20%) vs 7483 (+9%); cold TTFT 0.075 s vs 1.38 s vs 0.081 s. Qwen3.8-27B UD-IQ3_XXS: decode 60.3 vs 59.4 (+1.5%) vs 46.7 (+29%); prefill 1960 vs 1778 (+10%) vs 1412 (+39%); LU cold TTFT 11.2 s vs 0.15 s. Honest negative findings: PaRuX's static default flags are not faster than a bare `-c 8192 -ngl 99` launch (4B 178.9 vs 187.8, 27B 60.0 vs 61.5; q8_0 KV costs about 4-5% decode versus f16 KV), so a static default is not the advantage. The advantage is the automatic per-PC calibration: the existing "Optimize for this PC" pipeline run on the real 27B (workload coding) measured 11 candidates and applied f16 KV, context 32768 and embedded MTP speculative decoding: **125.3 decode tok/s versus 61 for the baseline (2.05x)**, draft accepted 82%, TTFT 325 ms, KV reuse +31.9%. A pass on the PaRuX engine with f16 KV and `--spec-type ngram-mod` measured, on the repetitive code-rewrite prompt, 4B 591.6 vs 185.4 tok/s (3.2x) and 27B 257.6 vs 61.3 (4.2x) with byte-identical output, and on essay prose 4B 342 vs 186 and 27B 81.7 vs 61.4 (the prose output text differs slightly from the baseline, as speculative batching changes greedy numerics; no quality claim is made). New in this build: a freshly downloaded chat GGUF is queued (persisted, 24 h lifetime) and the idle calibration runs after a 2-minute pause instead of 10 so the first real use is already tuned (`PostDownloadOptimizationTests`, `CalibrationAutomationTests`); calibration remains gated by Gaming Off, battery, GPU busy and user activity. Not measured: Ollama, other GPUs, non-CUDA backends, quality evaluation; these numbers say nothing about task quality.

**Release gate (owner instruction 2026-10-26):** before any release, every function must be tested on the real app through its UI (FlaUI/UIA on the real desktop, real models/runtimes), not only by unit or service tests. Items marked PARTIAL/NOT RUN above do not satisfy this gate.

**Items 3–5 — NOT RUN:** real Chat media workflows, comparable performance measurements, and clean VirtualBox install/upgrade/uninstall verification. Do not mark done without their requested real workflow/VM evidence.

### 2026-10-25 managed-runtime auto-update (PARTIAL: unit/contract tested with fakes; real download/GPU/live-launch NOT RUN)
Owner decision: PARUX_MANAGED llama.cpp runtimes (incl. the CUDA 12.4 -> 13.x move for Blackwell with driver >= 580) update automatically; models stay proposals; USER_MANAGED/SYSTEM_DETECTED are informational only. `ManagedRuntimeUpdater` (persisted state machine): driver/compute-capability-aware discovery -> stage-only install beside the active build (hash-verified, sidecar before atomic rename) -> smoke on the staged folder (real llama-server probe, `binary_only` fallback without a model) -> local benchmark gate (<= 5% slower prefill/decode, recorded in LocalBenchmarkHistory) -> atomic pointer switch only when no generation runs and Gaming is off -> probation on the first 2 real launches with automatic rollback -> retention of active + previous. Setting `KeepRuntimesUpdated` (default on) + Settings "Update runtimes now" + Runtime Manager active/previous/roll-back line. Open: live proof on the Blackwell host, probation wiring is launch-level (OOM-style failures also trigger rollback), no CPU/Vulkan-specific benchmark gate beyond the shared probe.
